- Promotions such as discount codes or sitewide sales should be created with a set start and end date and tested against margin before launch, so that they do not erode profit unintentionally. [L0430.012]
- Price the store at MAP and apply all discounts off MAP, which is the compare-at price. Never raise the price first and then discount it. *Older (2021).* [L1090.076]
- Average order value in the examples ranged from about $300 to about $1,000, and a minimum of about $200 was recommended as a starting threshold. *Older (2020).* [L1111.091]
- Minimum order values can be set so a code only applies above a threshold, for example 2000 dollars, which means 10% off orders above 2000 dollars. [L1322.006]

### Shopify setup

- Create the discount code in the Shopify admin before it is shown on any product page, because it has to work at checkout for shoppers who enter it in the cart. [L1192.004]
- Create any discount code in Shopify's discount section before you show it in the announcement bar or an exit-intent overlay. A code that does not exist in Shopify will not apply at checkout. *Older (2020).* [L1088.092]
- Procedure for a 5% order discount:
  1. Log in to the Shopify admin.
  2. Click Discounts in the left navigation.
  3. Click Create discount at the top.
  4. Select the discount type Amount off order (a discount on the total order amount).
  5. Name the code in the format save plus the number, for a 5% code the name is save five.
  6. Set the discount value to Percentage and enter 5%.
  7. Under customer eligibility leave the setting on All customers.
  8. Leave the minimum purchase at none and the usage limit unlimited.
  9. Leave the start date at the default, which is the day the code is created.
  10. Leave the end date empty unless the code is for a timed promotion such as Black Friday or Cyber Monday.
  11. Click Save and confirm the code shows as active in the Discounts list.

  [L1192.005]
- When a shopper enters the code in the cart at checkout, the percentage is deducted from the entire order total. [L1192.008]
- Customer eligibility can be limited to specific customer segments, such as a segment of past buyers, which allows a code for returning customers only. Specific customers can also be selected by their email address. [L1322.007]
- The combination option for other promotions controls whether several discounts can stack on one order. Keep it turned off unless stacking is intended, because the default start date is the current date and time. [L1322.009]
- The start date defaults to the date and time when the discount is created, so leaving it unchanged makes the code active immediately. An end date can be added to limit the code to a set window. [L1322.010]
- Set an end date for limited promotions such as Black Friday, Cyber Monday, or 4th of July, after which the code stops working. Without an end date, a time-limited promotion stays valid indefinitely. [L1322.011]
- Regular Shopify plans do not automatically end a sale price. Set the discount code to stop working at the sale's end time, for example midnight on the last day, or use an app. *2025.* [L0857.026]
- When creating a discount code in Shopify, choose the collections it is eligible for. Leave out collections containing products the discount may not apply to, such as products from vendors whose MAP policy forbids discounts. [L0837.033]

### Code naming

- Discount code names should reflect their value using the format save plus the number written out, so a 5% code is named `save five`. [L1192.006]
- Give every discount a named reason, such as a seasonal event or a Black Friday offer, so it looks realistic. A fixed-amount discount can also be named, for example as an $11 discount. [L1194.038]
- Name a discount code with a short word tied to the product or brand plus the percentage, so the shopper sees the saving in the code itself. The code in use was a 15% code with that format, and it was linked so the discount applies at checkout. [L0567.048]
- Code names used in the examples: `WELCOME10` for a 10% welcome code [L0607.010], `SAVE5` for a $5 pop-up code [L0634.005], `save five` for a $5 Klaviyo pop-up code [L1297.017], and `PARTNER5OFF` for an affiliate partner code [L0226.037].

### Limits and eligibility

- Limit sign-up coupon codes to one use per customer. Without this limit, a reusable code can be spread widely and erode your margin. [L0476.008]
- Discount codes that SMS apps generate automatically may be reusable by any customer by default, unlike the one-use-per-customer limit set in Shopify. Before launching an SMS discount flow, check the usage limit of each generated code so the offer does not spread beyond its intended audience and cost the store margin. [L0476.043]
- Maximum discount uses can be set to 5 uses in total, 10 uses in total, or once per customer. A code limited to one use in total can be redeemed by only one person. [L1322.008]
- Exclude customers who made recent purchases from discount promotions, specifically the most recent purchasers, to reduce complaints and buyer's remorse. [L0238.004]
- If the buyer's email address is not known, a personal code can be limited by a short end date instead of by naming the customer. Adding a single total use means only one person can redeem it. The short window also creates urgency for the buyer. [L1322.019]
- During peak sales, a discount code input can serve different customer segments with different codes, for example one code per customer tier. Each code entered in the cart shows its effect on the subtotal before checkout. [L1231.062]
- Keep a list of coupon codes the assistant may offer on its own, so standard discounts never need approval. Anything outside that list still requires the owner's approval. [L1000.057]
- Sample coupons: mark them for products that children could misuse as adults only, since children rarely mail coupons in. Pair this with a one-sample-per-household rule. [L1261.143]
- Do not announce coupon redemption dates in advance, because advance knowledge lets resellers buy up newspapers. Run such coupons in Sunday editions, which are harder to buy in bulk. [L1261.144]
- Military or first responder discount with low volume:
  1. Create a content page explaining the discount policy.
  2. Ask customers to email proof of service.
  3. Send a discount code after the proof is checked.

  No app is needed at this volume. *Older (2022).* [L1281.041]

### Expiry rules

- Coupon codes should include expiration dates to create urgency and motivate immediate purchase decisions. [L0040.012]
- A short validity window, such as 24 hours, creates urgency because the buyer must order before the code expires. Pairing the window with a personal code makes the offer feel specific to that buyer. [L1322.016]
- An end date is a fixed point in time. When a buyer is told when a code expires, state the date, the clock time and the time zone, for example midnight Eastern time. Saying only a relative word such as tomorrow leaves the deadline unclear. [L1322.020]
- Use one discount code with a fixed percentage that expires at a clear time on the same day, and link the button so the discount applies automatically at checkout. The offer in use was 15% off with a code that expires at midnight. [L0567.027]
- Strict expiry is not required on every offer. Coupons that look permanent lose their urgency, but a discount that is always "on sale" still works for buyers who use it now. [L1293.016]
- Example: a 15% code for first-time buyers that expires in 24 hours, shown in an exit popup. [L1400.005]
- Recurring expiring coupon codes, for example 5% or 10% off, rarely lead customers to see the discount as permanent. Returning customers are happy to use the code and save money. *Older (2019).* [L0960.038]
- Upfront expiring coupons and a bonus gift can be used to compete with a supplier's own website when you can match its pricing. *Older (2020).* [L0664.058]

### Expiring-code cycles

- Expire the promoted coupon code twice a week, typically at midnight on Wednesday and Sunday, and show a new code and offer for the next cycle. These specific days convert best; the source sets the times at midnight EST. [L0137.003, L0531.004]
- Update the code and the expiry text in the bar together each time. [L0531.004]
- In each expiring-code cycle the coupon code changes every time, but the discount percentage is only changed occasionally. A store that keeps the same percentage still has to refresh the code and the bar text on schedule. [L0531.021]
- Standard promotion: 10% off with an expiring coupon code, where the offer itself stays evergreen and only the coupon code and expiry date change. When one promotion ends, the next one starts with the same offer and a new code. *Older (2018).* [L1085.061]
- Expiring coupon promotions can run as a banner at the top of the store that ends on fixed weekdays, for example every Wednesday and Sunday. When one promotion ends, the next one starts right away with the same offer and a new coupon code, so the banner never goes empty. *Older (2018).* [L1085.100]
- Alternative rhythm: change coupons on a regular rhythm, such as every Tuesday and Thursday or every Saturday, and let them expire at least once a week. Do not make them expire every single day. [L1293.015]
- Expiring coupon block: it shows a coupon text such as 10% off and free shipping, followed by a coupon code field. The coupon code must already exist in Shopify before you enter it in the block, or it will not work for visitors. [L0976.019]
- Expiring coupon block settings: you can change the word before the date (default ends), set the number of days until the offer expires, and change the date format. The default date format can be switched to day-month-year for stores outside the US. [L0976.020]
- Dynamic Day Cart Coupon block: it requires a discount code that must be created in Shopify before it is configured. The block then takes three inputs: the discount code, the percentage off, and a custom text that describes the day-based deadline. [L1450.014]
- The block uses a day slot in its custom text, so the merchant writes the surrounding wording and the offer deadline day is shown dynamically in the cart. The merchant does not need to type a fixed weekday into the text each time. [L1450.020]
- A dynamic day coupon is not needed on a store without traffic. Hide or delete it at launch and add it once there is traffic, so its effect on conversion rates can be compared. [L1450.016]
- Meta ads: a promo code highlighted in the Meta ad setup appears to shoppers in the store and is applied automatically at checkout. Only use a code that will remain active permanently, such as an evergreen Shopify code, because an expired code breaks the running ads. [L1029.035]

### Coupon pages and promotion copy

- Create a coupon page on your store that lists current expiring coupons. Shoppers who are still comparing often search for these pages, so it can catch people who need one last push to buy. [L0920.010]
- Build a coupon page that lists your codes with a sense of urgency. The codes do not have to expire in reality; the page only needs to make the deal feel time-limited. Customers find it by searching your store name plus the word coupon, so an internal link is optional, and you can add a countdown timer. [L1357.010]
- Where the page states a specific end time to the buyer, the code must actually stop at that time. [L1322.020, L0857.026]
- Coupon pages can carry several offers, including product-specific codes. Savvy shoppers who search for coupons do not want to miss a deal, so an expiring offer is the final push to convert them. Test different versions to see which one works in your niche. [L1357.011]
- Coupon page copy pairs a light, friendly welcome with a deadline line. The welcome makes the visitor feel they found something exclusive, and the countdown line creates urgency to buy now.
  - *Example A:* You found our hidden savings page, so take 15 percent off before this code closes in 3 days.
  - *Example B:* Welcome to our insider deals list, and this bundle discount ends in 48 hours.

  [L1357.012]
- Discovery check: find discounts to use in this order:
  1. Review the discounts your top competitors run.
  2. Use low-value, tiered coupons rather than deep one-off discounts.

  [L0920.008]

### Welcome and first-order discounts

- Competitor stores offer a first-order discount of about 10 percent in exchange for an email signup. Use a signup popup or banner with this offer to build an email list at the same time. [L0600.020]
- The welcome kit discount for newsletter sign-ups is usually 5-10%, which customers see as the usual reward. Offer a collection link right after the coupon code. [L0605.029]
- Customer VIP sign-up discount example: 30% off an $80 product for customers who sign up for the launch list before it goes live. [L0605.034]
- Create the matching welcome discount in Shopify before building the sign-up form:
  1. In the Shopify store, open Discounts and click Create discount.
  2. Select Amount off order.
  3. Enter the discount code, for example WELCOME10.
  4. Set the value to 10 percent off.
  5. Set minimum requirements to no minimum.
  6. Limit the code to one use per customer.
  7. Click Save.
  8. Before creating a new code, check whether a suitable code already exists and reuse it.

  [L0607.010]
- Welcome series coupon: select a static coupon, enter the existing code (for example WELCOME10) and set it to 10 percent off. [L0607.020]
- Formula for a first-order offer: [discount amount] + [which order it applies to] + [time limit]. Naming all three makes the discount concrete and creates a deadline without vague promises. *Older (2021-22).* [L0571.035]
  - Example 1: *Take 10% off your first order, valid for the next 48 hours after you join the list.*
  - Example 2: *Get 15% off your first order, valid for 36 hours after you subscribe.*
- Do not offer a discount in the first two to three abandoned cart emails. Upfront discounts train customers to abandon carts and wait for rewards. Introduce the discount offer only after multiple high-value touchpoints (desire, pain, risk mitigation emails). [L0261.007]
- Use a two-tier welcome offer: email subscribers get 10% off sitewide, and SMS subscribers get an upgraded 15% code as a VIP reward. The higher tier for SMS rewards the channel that also needs consent. [L1470.007]

### Pop-up, SMS and email codes

- Klaviyo pop-up code, created in Shopify:
  1. Go to Discounts in the left menu and create a new discount.
  2. Enter a name, for example "save five".
  3. Choose fixed amount instead of percentage and enter $5.
  4. Optionally limit the code to one use per customer or set a minimum purchase, for example $50.
  5. Save the discount and give the code to the pop-up success step.

  [L1297.017]
- Clavio pop-up coupon, created in Shopify:
  1. In Shopify, go to Discounts and click Create discount.
  2. Choose Amount off order.
  3. Enter a code name such as SAVE5.
  4. Select a fixed amount of 5 dollars.
  5. Set the usage limit to one use per customer.
  6. Click Save discount, then enter the same code in the Clavio pop-up coupon field.

  [L0634.005]
- SMS discount link: build it as your store domain followed by /discount/ and the code name, for example storedomain.com/discount/CODE. The discount must be set to auto-apply at checkout, so the customer does not need to type the code. [L1470.009]
- Before going live, test both the SMS code and the email code at checkout with a real item in the cart. Confirm the discount is applied automatically and the amount matches the offer shown in the message. [L1470.012]
- Test a customer who has both an email code and an SMS code, and confirm at checkout that the 15% code replaces the 10% code instead of stacking. Run this test with a cart of realistic value, such as about $110 in the example, so the discounted total is visible before payment. [L1470.033]
- Use a benefit-selection popup that asks what the visitor wants (for example focus and memory, reduce inflammation, boost immunity, improve gut health) and reveals a mystery discount. Include an exit option that says no thanks and pay full price. [L1164.042]

### Shopping, ad and Merchant Center promotions

- Discount codes shown in Google Shopping ads appear as promotions. Advertising a discount on lower-margin products can cost margin without adding sales, so offer discounts only where the margin allows. *2025.* [L0472.057]
- Merchant Center promotions appear inside Shopping ads and are created in Google Merchant Center, not in Google Ads. They only become visible after the products have synced and the account is serving ads, so they will not show when the Merchant Center account is first created. [L0712.012]
- Create a Merchant Center promotion as follows:
  1. Open the promotions section in the left column of Merchant Center.
  2. Enter an ID name for the promotion.
  3. Select the country in which the promotion should be served.
  4. Enter the promotion details.
  5. Enter the redemption code the customer will use.
  6. Apply the promotion to specific products or to all products.

  [L0712.013]
- Promotions must be clear and testable: the coupon code must work on the store because Google bots try to enter it. Submit promotions in advance so they can be re-submitted if not approved. *Older (2022).* [L0665.065]
- Set up promotions in Google Merchant Center; percent-off promotions on products can be configured there. *Older (2022).* [L0665.066]
- Do not offer free shipping in Google Merchant Center promotions without a coupon code if the policy has changed. Instead, use a different promotion type such as a fixed-amount discount on a large order. [L1115.067]

### Offer strength by traffic source and product type

- Traffic from TikTok expects strong deals: buy-two-get-10-percent-off or buy-two-get-5-percent-off offers are weak. Audiences look for discounts of roughly 20, 30, 40 or 50 percent. [L1260.020]
- Traffic from TikTok expects strong deals, typically 20%, 30%, 40%, or 50% off. Small discounts such as 5% or 10% off usually do not work for this audience. [L1345.021]
- Strong offers that work on the store include buy one get one free, buy this and get this item free with the product, and a maximum discount of 70 percent off; 50 percent off is the usual baseline. Electronics may not suit buy one get one free, while items like jewelry often suit it. [L1382.017]
- Bundle offer: buy two items and receive a fixed discount of 14 dollars. A separate code of 5 dollars was offered for customers who wanted to buy only one item, and both codes were used a lot. [L1439.033]
- Blanket example: the store offered a 30 unit discount on the blanket, with free shipping within Switzerland and delivery in 2 to 3 days from local stock. A buy-two offer saved 14, and a single-item code saved 5. [L0492.032]
- Partner and backlink coupon strategy:
  1. Create exclusive coupon codes for affiliate partners (e.g., `PARTNER5OFF`).
  2. List coupons on coupon aggregator websites in your niche.
  3. Use free shipping coupons, percentage discounts (5% off), or holiday-specific codes.
  4. Monitor which coupons drive conversions and repeat best performers.
  5. Some aggregator sites provide backlinks; prioritize those.

  [L0226.037]
- Launch discount strategy: offer a 50% off coupon to generate rapid sales. Example: a 50% off coupon resulted in 402 sales in 2 weeks at break-even price. Benefits: drives rankings, generates reviews, creates repeat customers (especially for consumables like supplements). [L0227.027]
- Asking a supplier to include custom coupon cards in shipments is allowed. Some suppliers agree and others do not have the resources, so the request is worth making. *Older (2018).* [L1085.072]

### Where sources disagree

- **Expiry cadence.** Wednesday and Sunday midnight is the twice-weekly cycle in the Source A material [L0137.003, L0531.004, L1085.100]. The Tuesday/Thursday or Saturday rhythm is an alternative, with the same rule of at least one expiry per week and never daily [L1293.015]. Use the Wednesday and Sunday cycle when running a banner that refreshes twice weekly; use the alternative when a store wants a different rhythm.
- **Expiry on every offer.** Expiration dates create urgency [L0040.012]. Strict expiry is not required on every offer, because always-on offers still work for buyers who use them now [L1293.016]. Use strict expiry for time-limited campaigns and dated promotions; use always-on offers for standing benefits.
- **Coupon-page deadlines.** A coupon page only needs to feel time-limited, and the codes need not expire in reality [L1357.010]. Where the page or code states a specific end time, the code must stop at that time [L1322.020, L0857.026].
- **Percentage versus dollar-off.** Percentage is the default [L1404.016] and the usual Shopify choice [L1322.005]. Dollar-off suits pop-ups and emails and higher-AOV or bundle offers [L0476.004, L0232.003]. Both are roughly equivalent in effect [L1404.014].
- **Size of discount.** The starting test size is 5% [L1192.007]. Coupons are commonly 5-10% [L0137.002]. Volume and traffic offers need 20-50% [L1259.029, L1260.020]. Each figure depends on context: test size, competitor baseline, or audience expectation. Margin decides whether any of them is affordable [L1097.027, L1148.026].
- **Standing sitewide discounts.** A standing discount erodes margin [L0409.028]. A sitewide 10% reduced to 5% keeps a site-wide discount while protecting margin [L1097.028]. An expiring sitewide code is an accepted format [L1085.061].
- **Minimum order values.** Course A's example recommended a starting threshold of about $200 at AOVs of $300-$1,000 *(older, 2020)* [L1111.091]. The Shopify example sets $2,000 as the threshold [L1322.006]. Choose the threshold to match your own AOV.
- **Free shipping in promotions.** Free shipping is an option on coupons [L0226.037]. Under Merchant Center policy changes, free shipping is not offered without a coupon code [L1115.067].

## Checking supplier rules before discounting

### Read the supplier agreement before any discount

Before creating any discount code or running any promotion, work through each supplier's reseller agreement:

1. Review each supplier's reseller agreement.
2. Document which suppliers:
   - prohibit discounts entirely,
   - allow coupon-code-based discounts (applied after a code is entered),
   - allow no restrictions.
3. Apply only permitted discounts to products from each supplier. [L0251.060]

- Read each supplier's MAP policy carefully and follow its specific rules before creating store-wide discount codes. Some suppliers explicitly prohibit all discount codes in their MAP policy. [L0276.002]
- Check the MAP policy before offering coupons. Many MAP suppliers allow coupons, but some do not, so adjust the exit-intent offer for those products. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0247.042]
- Whether a discount such as a coupon on a MAP-priced item is allowed depends on each supplier's policy, so read the MAP policy you agreed to with every supplier. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L0356.034]
- Check each brand's MAP agreement before offering coupons or special discounts. If the agreement forbids coupon codes or special offers on a product, leave those offers off that product. If it is silent, a discount is usually allowed, but confirm with the supplier because they may later object. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0358.012]
- Check the policies you signed during approval before running any coupon. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0853.084]
- Check the MAP clause in the supplier agreement before offering coupon codes, because the agreement states whether coupons are forbidden. [L1094.074]
- Most suppliers allow discounts on MAP products. Confirm this in the authorized retailer agreement, because some suppliers forbid discounts in writing. [L0857.020]
- Some supplier agreements forbid coupon codes or discounts entirely, so check the agreement before offering them. [L1113.074]
- Coupon codes are not allowed for a product if the supplier agreement explicitly forbids discounts on it. Check the agreement before offering any discount. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0963.063]
- Check each supplier agreement for discount restrictions. Coupon codes must be entered manually by customers (see the section on advertising below). [L0251.020]
- Costs such as the supplier's MAP policy must be checked before any bonus or discount is launched on a supplier's products. [L0855.054]

### How common discount restrictions are

Sources give different rough shares. They come from different years and are estimates, so for any single brand the agreement decides.

| Share of suppliers | Meaning | Source year | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| About 90% or more allow discount codes | Most MAP suppliers allow codes. *(Older advice, 2021.)* | 2021 | L0671.049, L0671.188 |
| About 95% allow discount codes the customer enters at checkout | Only while the advertised price stays at or above MAP. Some agreements forbid codes entirely. *(Older advice, 2022.)* | 2022 | L1113.087 |
| Most allow; roughly 5% or fewer forbid codes | For suppliers that forbid codes, leave their products out of the sale. *(Older advice, 2021.)* | 2021 | L1279.052 |
| A small number forbid any discount on their brand, even at checkout | Check each brand's MAP policy and skip discounts for brands that forbid them. | 2025 | L0972.038 |
| Rarely, some forbid coupon codes | Exclude those supplier products from coupon offers. *(Older advice, 2019.)* | 2019 | L1110.012 |

### Coupon codes: what is normally allowed

- Discount codes can be offered to customers unless the supplier agreement specifically forbids them. The discount must not be applied to the advertised price on the website. List the product at MAP and apply the discount only through a code at checkout. [L0278.029]
- Discount codes are normally permitted under MAP, for example a 10 percent off code at checkout that lowers the price after the discount is applied. This is allowed unless the supplier's MAP agreement says no discounts. In that case, exclude those products from coupons. [L0667.064]
- A coupon applied at checkout is generally fine under MAP, because the advertised price stays at MAP. A few supplier agreements prohibit any discount below MAP, and those products must not be discounted. [L0966.040]
- Most suppliers' MAP policies forbid advertising or displaying a product below MAP. A coupon code that lowers the price at checkout does not break this rule, because the advertised price stays at MAP. [L0972.037]
- Discount codes can take the checkout price below MAP unless the supplier prohibits discounts. The advertised price on the product page and in Google Shopping must stay at MAP. [L1281.032]
- Use promo codes instead of marked-down prices, so that the listed price stays at MAP. A coupon discounts the price at checkout unless the supplier agreement specifically forbids coupon codes. [L1094.073]
- MAP (minimum advertised price) is set by the supplier and limits the lowest price at which the product may be advertised. A discount code that lowers the final price is generally not a violation because the advertised price stays at or above MAP, but each supplier's agreement must be read because a few forbid codes. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L1069.041]
- For a MAP product, a sale must be run through a coupon code rather than by lowering the displayed price on the product. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L1069.042]
- Usually you cannot offer discounts such as 10 to 15 percent off MAP products. Some supplier MAP agreements forbid coupon codes, which is rare; if the agreement does, exclude those supplier products from your coupon offers. *(Older advice, 2019.)* [L1110.012]
- Discounts that are not advertised are generally allowed under MAP pricing unless the supplier explicitly forbids them. Advertising a price below MAP is a separate issue and can violate the supplier's policy. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0801.046]

**Conflict on 10-15 percent discounts.** The 2019 unit (L1110.012) says you usually cannot offer 10 to 15 percent off MAP products. Units from 2021 to 2025 (L0667.064, L0966.040, L0972.037) say discount codes are normally permitted as long as the advertised price stays at MAP. Use the later view for checkout codes. The 2019 caution applies only to a supplier whose agreement forbids discounts.

### Keep the advertised price at MAP

**Principle.** Sell at MAP and treat any extra offer as a discount off MAP. Do not inflate the MAP price to create room for a discount. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0247.037]

- List products at MAP and fund discounts from the margin you can afford. Do not list above MAP, because products above MAP lose traction on Google Shopping. If a discount is too large, reduce the discount rather than raising the list price. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0968.075]
- If the margin cannot cover discounts, list the product at MAP without discount codes for that supplier. Listing a higher price lowers clicks in Google Shopping because the product appears at the highest price. [L1283.076]
- If you must choose between pricing above MAP while offering discounts and pricing at MAP without discounts, the MAP-without-discounts option keeps your prices in line with other sellers on Google Shopping and avoids looking more expensive. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0801.047]
- If MAP is the minimum advertised price, discount codes can take the checkout price below MAP. List the product at MAP and apply discount codes to the MAP price, rather than inflating the price and then offering a discount. [L1283.075]
- Under MAP pricing, keep everyday prices at the MAP level and run promotions as a percentage off that price, such as 5% or 10%. Raising a price and then discounting it back to MAP is an alternative, but it changes the displayed price. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0853.015]

**Conflict on inflating the price.** L0853.015 describes raising the price and discounting back to MAP as an alternative. L0247.037, L0968.075 and L1283.075 advise against inflating. Default to listing at MAP. Use the alternative only if you accept that the displayed price changes.

#### Worked examples: what the customer sees

| Case | Price on product page and in ads | Price after code at checkout | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| MAP $1,000, $50 code | $1,000 | $950 | L0276.003, L1281.032 |
| MAP $1,000, $50 code for orders over $1,000 | $1,000 (may not be advertised at $950) | $950, as the code is entered | L0857.021 |
| MAP $1,000, $100 code | $1,000 | $900 | L0963.062 |
| $1,000 item, $50 coupon | $1,000. Showing $950 would break MAP. | $950 | L1094.075 |
| MAP $1,000, $900 after coupon | Cannot be advertised at $900 in ads or emails | $900 | L0964.018 |
| MAP $1,000, 10% off code | $1,000 until the code is entered | Discounted price after code | L1279.053 |

- A product with a MAP of $1,000 must show $1,000 on the product page. A $50 code for orders over $1,000 may lower the checkout total to $950, but the store cannot advertise the product at $950 without the code. [L0857.021]
- Lowering the displayed price to $950 on a $1,000 item with a $50 coupon would break MAP. The product remains advertised at $1,000 and the coupon is applied at checkout. [L1094.075]

### Where prices may appear

- Google ads, Facebook ads and strikethrough pricing may not show a product below its MAP. Most brands allow discount codes the customer enters at checkout, but these must not be auto-applied or advertised as the lower price. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L1113.073]
- Coupon codes must be entered manually by customers. Do not auto-apply discounts at checkout. [L0251.020]
- When a coupon code is allowed, show the full price on the product page and reveal the discounted price only after the code is entered. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L1279.053]
- Offer discounts and coupon codes on product pages only where the supplier's MAP agreement permits it. If the supplier forbids discounting below MAP, do not discount those product pages. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0852.051]
- For special supplier pricing during a sale, put the discounted products on a separate bundle page rather than changing the price on existing product pages. [L1094.077]

### Brand-level exclusions

- Some suppliers forbid coupon codes that push the price below MAP. If a supplier's agreement says so, exclude that supplier's products from any coupon code that lowers the price. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0961.003]
- Do not discount below MAP if the supplier's policy forbids it. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0853.084]
- If a supplier forbids discounts or coupons, exclude its products from coupon offers and offer a bonus gift instead, so the deal stays attractive without breaking the policy. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0665.063]
- Before offering discount codes on a supplier's products, read its MAP agreement. If it forbids discounts, do not discount that supplier's products. Most suppliers, about 90% or more, allow discount codes. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0671.049, L0671.188]

### Store-wide and multi-brand sales

- For a store-wide sale, include only brands that agreed to join the promotion. Use the announcement bar's advanced targeting to hide it on pages of vendors that are not part of the sale. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0671.052, L0671.191]
- Use notification bar promotions only for vendors that agreed to the promotion. Show promotions based on conditional rules, not as blanket offers. *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0671.057]
- To run promotions across brands with different discount levels: *(Older advice, 2021.)* [L0853.083]
  1. Change the promotion banner on each product page to show the offer relevant to that product's brand or collection.
  2. Create a separate coupon code for a brand only if it needs one.
  3. Check the supplier's policy first, and exclude any brand that forbids coupon discounts from the promotion.

### Adding value without changing the price

**Principle.** When price discounts are not possible, add value through bonus gifts, extended warranties or expedited shipping. A bonus gift can carry more perceived value to the customer than its cost, and extended warranties can cost little or nothing when arranged with the supplier. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0852.052]

**Principle.** Discounts on products that suppliers do not allow to be discounted can be replaced with other value, such as expedited shipping, free white glove delivery or a small bonus gift. Check each option's margin before offering it. [L0804.090]

| Option | Changes the product price? | Notes | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Free expedited shipping | No | Can be run as a spend threshold, for example once the customer spends over $500, $1,000 or $2,000 | L0837.034, L0852.052 |
| Upgraded shipping, such as white glove delivery | No | Check each supplier's MAP agreement, because a brand can have separate rules outside MAP | L0961.002, L0804.090 |
| Bonus gift from a third party | No | Gifts, free shipping and upgraded shipping do not change the price, so they do not violate MAP *(older advice, 2020)* | L0961.002, L0852.052 |
| Free bonus product from the same supplier | Can violate MAP | Get written approval from the supplier before running the promotion | L0855.049 |
| Extended warranty | No | Can cost little or nothing when arranged with the supplier | L0852.052 |

- If a supplier's MAP policy forbids discount codes, run promotions that do not change the price, such as free expedited shipping or a free bonus gift once a customer spends over $500, $1,000 or $2,000. [L0837.034]
- Bonus gifts, free shipping and upgraded shipping such as white glove delivery do not change the product price, so they do not violate MAP. A brand can still have separate rules outside MAP, so check each supplier's agreement. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0961.002]
- A free bonus product from the same supplier can violate MAP. Get written approval from the supplier before running the promotion. [L0855.049]
- For a supplier that forbids discounts, a bonus gift instead of a discount makes the deal more attractive without breaking the policy. *(Older advice, 2022.)* [L0665.063]

### Check margins before offering a discount

- Do not sell at MAP with a 10 percent discount. Net margins around 15 percent are too low unless the order value is thousands of dollars. Try a 5 percent discount, a smaller discount, or a bonus gift that costs less than the discount would. [L0806.054]
- Check the margin of each option before offering it. [L0804.090]
- A coupon is paid by the retailer, not the supplier. The discount comes out of the retailer's profit, not the wholesale price. The supplier still receives the wholesale price. [L0667.065]
- If the margin cannot cover discounts, list the product at MAP without discount codes for that supplier. [L1283.076]

### Negotiate supplier-funded promotions

**Principle.** If product margins are insufficient for discounts or coupons, negotiate with suppliers for promotional support. Propose a promotion where the supplier includes a free item of value, worth $20 to $50 depending on the product price, with customer purchases. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0245.050]

**Rule.** When product pricing is fixed and margins are low, build tighter relationships with suppliers to access co-promotional opportunities. The supplier includes a free premium, upgraded shipping or a discount, instead of you absorbing promotional costs yourself. *(Older advice, 2020.)* [L0245.051]

**Example.** Buy-one-get-one offers were easier to source when suppliers had excess inventory. Today they are mainly worth offering on stock that is overstocked or being discontinued, as described in the supplier procedure. [L1204.027]

**Procedure.** Sourcing a supplier-funded deal, such as buy-one-get-one or a deep discount:

1. Review supplier inventory lists regularly and look for SKUs with very high stock that are not moving.
2. Watch for supplier emails announcing that a SKU is being discontinued.
3. Ask the supplier which promotional price they can give, for example a buy-one-get-one price, a 30 to 50 percent discount on the SKU, or a temporary waiver of the MAP restriction for the promotion.
4. Run the offer only if the numbers show it is profitable for your store.

[L1204.028]

**Competitor offers.** Competitor holiday offers can be checked with the supplier before you copy them. Send the supplier a screenshot of the competitor's offer and ask whether it violates policy or whether the same promotion is available to you. [L1283.077]

## Free shipping, shipping fees and delivery upgrades

### Free shipping as the default offer

- Free shipping is standard practice in dropshipping, but every shipping cost still reduces your profit margin. [L0016.003]
- Free shipping is the default in this model, so each product's shipping cost must be covered by its sale price. Drop or reprice any product whose price cannot absorb its shipping cost before listing it. [L1085.101]
- Suppliers never offer free shipping. When a store advertises free shipping, the store pays and the customer does not. This is the standard approach for about 99% of stores. [L1274.072]
- Free shipping means the merchant pays the shipping cost, not the customer. That cost must be built into the margin calculation for each product. [L0966.050, L0276.013]
- Free shipping can be offered on everything, with extra charges only for special services such as white glove delivery. (older, 2019) [L0356.036]
- Offer free standard shipping on all products, with the shipping cost built into the product price, so customers see free shipping and the cost is covered in the margin. (older, 2021) [L1279.055]
- Shipping and product prices should be set so the store can offer free shipping and still keep a profit. The customer does not pay shipping, but the supplier's shipping cost must be included in the margin math. (older, 2020) [L1111.092]
- Free shipping on almost everything means a shipping app connected to a UPS account is not needed. Put shipping costs into the margin calculation instead. (older, 2020) [L0664.056]
- Shipping is not a separate Shopify charge. When the store offers free shipping and pays for it, the built-in Shopify shipping system is not used. (older, 2022) [L0969.003]
- Free shipping is often overlooked, but cost-conscious shoppers prefer a store with free delivery over one that charges a few dollars. Absorbing the delivery cost can make your store the first choice over competitors. [L1399.012]
- Offer free shipping and build it into the product price, because a visible shipping charge makes many buyers abandon the purchase. Buyers accept a higher product price more easily than an extra shipping fee. [L0661.022]
- For beginners, build shipping into the product price and offer free shipping rather than charging a separate fee, since separate shipping charges are more likely to cause abandoned checkouts, especially on low-priced products. [L1172.053]
- Showing a $5 shipping fee only at checkout can cause buyers to abandon. Offer free shipping, then sell faster shipping as a paid upsell. [L0352.029]

### When to offer free shipping

- Free shipping is only worth offering when the margin covers the shipping cost. [L0855.053, L0941.053]
- Free shipping works on expensive products but loses money on cheap ones of the same size and weight. A three-seater sofa sold at $1,500 can carry free shipping with healthy margin; the same sofa at $500 cannot. [L0429.043]
- Free shipping on heavy items is not standard practice. Check what competitors do first, and offer free shipping only when the margin still covers the freight cost. [L0801.044]
- Free shipping is very hard to keep profitable when MAP is less than 2x wholesale. [L0963.066]
- Free shipping and a price-match guarantee are common in this market, but both costs come out of the spread. Offer them only on products where the spread after average shipping still covers ad costs and merchant fees; otherwise they can erase the margin. [L0854.115]
- Do not list products whose shipping costs make free shipping unprofitable. For the guitar example, list only products priced above $200 when shipping is expensive. [L0665.062]
- At the first click, price usually matters more than shipping, because customers see shipping only later in checkout. High shipping can still send them back to look for other options, so balance both. [L0470.048]
- Before offering free shipping, calculate how it changes your margin on each sale, because it is a cost of the sale. Free shipping works as a purchase incentive, so weigh the margin impact against the extra sales it may bring. (Source B, older, 2021-22) [L1480.020]
- Use free shipping only after checking shipping rates. Lower shipping costs by comparing carriers, shipping direct from suppliers, or getting multiple free quotes. [L1094.083]

#### Deciding whether to keep free shipping when costs are high

Older advice (2019) gives this sequence:

1. Calculate the profit left per order as a percentage of the sale price, including shipping costs.
2. If the margin is only about 5% to 15%, work on the margin with the supplier first, by asking for better product prices or shipping rates.
3. Compare with competitor offers: if they ship free, charging for shipping will reduce conversions noticeably.
4. Only move to paid shipping if the margin cannot be fixed and the product still makes sense.

[L1069.050]

#### Competitor-based rules

- If all competitors offer free shipping, charging a shipping fee usually makes the sale harder. Cover the cost by negotiating shipping terms with suppliers or product terms, not by charging extra. (older, 2019) [L0960.037]
- If all competitors charge for shipping, charging for shipping too is reasonable. Adding a shipping cost into the product price makes the listing look more expensive in ads and reduces clicks. [L0970.108]
- If competitors in a specialized niche charge for shipping, you can charge for shipping as well. This is an exception to the standard free-shipping approach. (2018) [L1274.073]
- Check competitors' shipping policies before deciding how to price shipping, because shoppers see competitor prices and shipping charges side by side in ads. [L0966.051]
- Shipping on large items can be charged to the customer if the market does so, but check this against competitor pricing and whether shipping is built into the price. [L0967.075]

### Calculating freight cost

#### Freight Center procedure (older, 2022)

To calculate shipping costs for a free-shipping offer:

1. Use Freight Center dot com.
2. Enter the supplier warehouse location.
3. Enter product weight and dimensions.
4. Get shipping quotes to a sample city in California, Florida, and Texas.
5. Average these quotes to determine typical shipping expense.
6. Factor this into product pricing.

[L0249.035]

#### Weight-based formula

Weight-based shipping rate = product weight × rate per pound. For example, at $2 per pound, a 50 lb product costs $100 to ship. Product weights must be accurate for this to work. (older, 2021) [L0671.078]

#### Calculated shipping at checkout

- If shipping costs vary and you do not want to build them into product prices, a Shopify shipping app can charge calculated shipping at checkout. This keeps profit figures closer to the real freight cost of each order. (2024) [L0806.110]
- Charge shipping per brand by enabling shipping in Shopify or by adding a surcharge to the product price, for example a $100 product with a $10 shipping surcharge. (older, 2019) [L0663.032]
- When a supplier has high shipping costs, such as an average of $600, either build the shipping into the product price or charge a brand-specific shipping fee. The choice depends on what competitors do. (2025) [L0857.029]
- Shopify requires you to offer either free shipping or paid shipping, not both. Product-specific shipping upgrades must therefore be sold as product options. (older, 2021) [L0247.035]
- Shopify shipping rules as described in the 2019 course: free shipping over $200 applies to all products $200 and above if the free-shipping rule is activated. For products under $200, configure separate shipping rules: charge a fixed amount per order, or enable real-time calculated shipping based on weight and box dimensions. (older, 2019) [L0243.038]

> **Note on conflicting Shopify sources:** The 2019 setup (free rule for $200 and above, separate rules below) and the 2021 statement (either free or paid, not both) both describe Shopify behavior from older courses. Check current Shopify settings before relying on either. [L0243.038, L0247.035]

### Free-shipping thresholds

Sources give different thresholds. Each applies to a different model:

| Source | Threshold | Context | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source A (older, 2019) | $200 | Standard minimum order value for free shipping on dropship products | L0243.015 |
| Source A (older, 2022) | $100 | Example text: "Free shipping to the continental US on all orders over $100," shown on the product page and in the shipping tab | L1281.016 |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | $500 | Start with free shipping on all orders; if margins are too thin, add a $50 flat fee below $500, editable later in Shopify | L0731.015 |
| Source C | $39 | Example store: free shipping above $39, otherwise a $9.99 fee | L1172.023, L1176.025 |
| Source C | 19 euros | Threshold used by a German store, adoptable in another market | L1161.033 |
| Source D (2026) | $25 | Single at $19, two-pack at $29.99 | L0861.006 |

- Use free shipping on all products instead of a threshold, and keep low-priced products out of the catalog so the store focuses on higher-priced items. (older, 2019) [L1069.051]
- Free shipping can be offered for a limited day or week, or through a discount code that activates it. For example, normal shipping of $7 can be shown as free for one day only. (Source C) [L0502.015]
- Free shipping over $75 on a product priced at $85 means every order ships free. The lesson states this only really works for clearance items, because those products are already sold at a low margin. [L0790.024]

#### Setting the threshold in the cart

- Set the free-shipping threshold above the single-unit price and below the bundle total, so a customer who buys two qualifies for free shipping. The example uses a single at $19 and a two-pack at $29.99 with a threshold of $25. (Source D, 2026) [L0861.006]
- Set the threshold between the price of one item and the price of two, so the customer gets free shipping only by buying two. A threshold above the single-item price but at or below the two-unit total is what pushes the buyer up the ladder. (Source D, 2026) [L0862.004]
- A very low product price, such as $9.99, is often a sign that the store charges for shipping separately. Check the store header and checkout for a free-shipping threshold before judging the price. [L1172.022]
- In the lesson's example, the threshold was $39, so customers were encouraged to add items until the cart reached $39. Bundles that reach the threshold in one purchase remove the friction of adding items. [L1172.023]
- Pairing a single item with a complementary trending product as a bundle can lift the total order past the threshold. For example, one item at $19.99 or a bundle with a second product at $29.99 keeps shipping free and preserves margin. [L1172.025]

### Progress bars and threshold display

#### Configuring the free-shipping bar in Shopify

1. Go to Header > Free shipping bar.
2. Turn on Show free shipping bar.
3. Set the background color.
4. Enter the threshold amount, for example 1000 dollars.

The cart then reads "spend X to unlock free shipping" and updates as the cart total changes. [L0775.028, L1309.042]

#### Remaining-amount formula

Amount shown on the bar = threshold − current cart total. For example, 1000 − 799 = 201. If the bar shows a different number, check the cart price against the threshold. [L0775.029]

With a threshold of 1000 and a 799 product in the cart, the bar shows that $201 more is needed, and it updates automatically as items are added. [L1309.043]

- A free-shipping bar is only useful when a free-shipping offer exists. If all products ship free, it can stay off. [L1309.044]
- In the cart drawer, show the threshold with the remaining amount. For example, if the cart is under $40, display "add $20 more to get free shipping." This nudges the customer to add an item that raises the order value. (Source C) [L1412.016]
- Show the free-shipping threshold as a set amount next to Add to Cart, not as a vague line such as "qualifying once the threshold is met." Vague shipping wording invites questions at checkout. (Source D, 2026) [L1188.059]
- Shipping options left open or enabled while ads are running can let customers order with a shipping setup that was never intended. Check the shipping settings before launching any paid campaign. (Source C) [L1172.054]

### Charging for shipping on low-ticket items

#### Example rule for low-priced products

- Example store rule: free shipping on orders above $39, otherwise a $9.99 shipping fee is charged. Shoppers who want free shipping must buy several items to reach the threshold. (Source C) [L1176.025]
- For low-priced products (for example $9 or $14), charge a separate shipping fee to keep the margin, then offer free shipping above a higher cart threshold such as $30 or $40 to raise average order value. Example of your own: a $12 gadget with a $7.99 shipping fee, plus free shipping above $35. (Source C) [L1176.026]
- If a product's selling price is low and the margin is thin, raise the price or charge a shipping fee, and pair it with an offer that encourages buying two. (Source C) [L1176.028]

#### Alternatives to a separate fee

- If many dropshippers charge separately, a fee of about $5 to $7 per order is common. Customers who see shipping at checkout may abandon the order if the fee is too high. (Source C) [L1172.028]
- If many customers start checkout but do not complete it because shipping costs appear, add about $7 to the product price and offer free shipping. This applies when the conversion rate stays poor despite good ad traffic. (Source C) [L1172.029]
- Charging for shipping while running ads against free-shipping competitors almost never converts. Ads should only go to products where the margin is high enough to absorb the freight. (older, 2023) [L0855.044]

### Heavy and large items

#### Margin requirements

- For large or heavy products, the spread between MAP and wholesale must cover shipping, which can be about $250 per item. Such products should sell for roughly $1,000 or more, with wholesale at half or less of that price, before free shipping is viable. Inexpensive heavy items are a loss. (older, 2022) [L0429.042]
- Items that are large or heavy can still be sold if the price is high enough to cover shipping and leave a healthy margin after free shipping. (older, 2021) [L0671.131, L0671.266]
- Example: $300 margin left after wholesale on a product with $250 freight leaves only $50 profit. Charging the customer $250 for shipping keeps the $300 margin at a total of $550, but only works if competitors also charge for shipping. (2023) [L0855.043]
- Free shipping on products that cost a lot to freight is only worth offering if the margin still covers the shipping cost. (2023) [L0855.053]

#### Listing heavy items with a shipping add-on

- For heavy items where freight is expensive, one method is to list the product at a lower price and add a required shipping add-on at checkout. For example, a $500 product with a required $300 shipping add-on reaches $800, while the advertised product price stays at $500. (older, 2021) [L0801.042]
- Listing a heavy item at $800 with shipping included in the price usually reduces clicks when the competitor shows $500 with freight calculated later. The lower advertised price with a required shipping add-on keeps the price visible while covering shipping costs. (older, 2021) [L0801.043]
- For non-MAP products with high shipping costs, raise the product price to include shipping and show free shipping. Customers see the same total at checkout. [L0941.053]

#### Matching competitor prices on heavy items (older, 2023)

If competitors charge shipping on heavy items and you need to match their price while keeping the item competitive:

1. Set your listed price in line with the competitors' prices for those products.
2. Create a separate shipping profile in Shopify and add the heavy products to it.
3. Set that profile so the products are not eligible for free shipping.

This keeps the price competitive for Google Shopping traffic while shipping is charged separately. [L0470.031]

#### Supplier fees and packages

- Supplier shipping fees should be built into the product price rather than shown as a separate fee. Avoid asterisk shipping explanations on product pages. (older, 2021) [L0962.102]
- When a supplier insists on charging shipping, negotiate shipping and warranty into a single package price. This gives customers one price and supports the store's extended warranty offers. (older, 2021) [L0962.103]
- Shipping to Hawaii is usually not worth the effort, because costs are so high that most customers will not pay them. (older, 2022) [L0963.067], see the Alaska and Hawaii section below.

### Excluding Alaska and Hawaii

Sources differ on how to handle these states. Both approaches are shown:

| Approach | Source | Applies when |
|---|---|---|
| Charge a separate surcharge or flat fee for these areas | Source A (older, 2021), L0270.018 | You want to ship there and cover the higher cost with a fee |
| Remove them from checkout options and offer a quote by contact | Source A (older, 2019), L1069.052; Source A (older, 2022), L1281.015 | Shipping is too expensive for most products |
| Do not advertise to Hawaii for most products | Source A (older, 2022), L0963.067 | Most products |

- Offer free shipping to the continental US only. Hawaii and Alaska are excluded from free shipping by default because shipping there is very expensive. (older, 2022) [L1281.014]
- For Alaska and Hawaii, do not offer free shipping. Remove these states from checkout options and link a contact address on the contact page so customers can request a quote that includes a surcharge. (older, 2019) [L1069.052]

#### Exclusion procedure (older, 2022)

To exclude Hawaii and Alaska from standard free shipping:

1. In the shipping rules, make Hawaii and Alaska unavailable to select at checkout.
2. On the shipping policy page, say that customers who want to ship there can contact the store for a quote.
3. Handle those quote requests manually.

[L1281.015]

- Free shipping message example: "Free shipping to the continental US on all orders over $100." The threshold is shown on the product page and in the shipping tab. (older, 2022) [L1281.016]
- Free shipping to the continental US is the policy in the 2021 course. Alaska and Hawaii shipping is significantly more expensive, so charge a separate surcharge or flat fee for these areas. (older, 2021) [L0270.018]

### Expedited shipping

#### Setting up an expedited option (older, 2019)

1. Offer expedited shipping only with suppliers who can reliably ship fast and with whom you have a strong relationship.
2. Add expedited shipping as an optional toggle on product pages (for example, "Expedited shipping-ships in 24 hours: yes or no").
3. Charge a fixed add-on fee if the customer selects the expedited option (for example, $50).

[L0243.029]

- Priority shipping or priority processing can be offered as an add-on fee of about 10% of the product price (for example $2.95 to $3.95) to add extra margin. (Source C) [L1436.048]
- If competitors deliver in 5 to 10 days, arrange with the supplier to dispatch within 24 hours and deliver in 3 to 5 days. Faster delivery is a visible value that helps the store stand out. (older, 2020) [L0852.053]
- Free expedited shipping is a monetary promotion where the store waives a fee it normally charges, typically worth $25 to $50 for expedited shipping. Orders above a set amount placed by a set date ship expedited for free. It works well just before Christmas, when gift buyers pay attention to shipping speed. The offer can be limited to certain suppliers or to orders above a price threshold. [L1205.008]
- Shopify's default checkout does not allow easy assignment of specific shipping offers to specific products. Use the product upsell app for same-day shipping instead of the shipping page; the upsell app can target each offer to specific products. [L0923.045]

### White glove delivery and assembly

#### Ways to offer white glove

White glove can be placed in three ways: free on product pages to lift conversion, as a paid shipping upgrade at checkout, or as a custom quote requested before or after the order. The right choice depends on the niche and on whether margins are high enough to absorb it. (older, 2022) [L1281.002]

- Offering free white glove on high-ticket products only makes sense when the product margin is high enough to pay for it. If competitors do not offer the same service, showing it on the product page can noticeably raise conversion rates. (older, 2022) [L1281.003]
- White glove delivery can be sold as a paid upgrade, or given away as a free bonus for very expensive products when it helps win sales in the niche. Compare several white glove providers, since prices vary widely, and choose one with good reviews and the lowest cost. (older, 2020) [L0664.088]
- For a custom desk that sells for $5,000 where three competitors use standard delivery, offering free white glove delivery (costing $100 to $300 extra) can win the sale. Each sale won this way adds the margin of that order, so the extra cost can pay off. (older, 2020) [L0961.006]
- A low-cost paid option at checkout is white glove delivery sold as an upgrade the customer can add to the order. Use this when your margins cannot absorb a free version. (older, 2020) [L0961.008]
- Shipping upgrades such as white-glove delivery, for example $50 to $80 extra, can be used as perceived value on higher-priced products. This can justify a price above a competitor that does not offer them. (older, 2019) [L1002.036]
- Do not apply a flat markup for premium services such as White Glove across all orders. Costs differ by supplier and product, so a flat charge can lose money. Offer these services per product page. (older, 2021) [L0247.036]
- Do not charge flat shipping fees above the product price unless offering an upgraded service such as expedited shipping, white-glove delivery, or installation. Flat fees without added value don't match competitor offerings. (older, 2022) [L0272.024]
- Free white glove delivery suits products where the customer values installation, not just curbside drop-off. Free extended warranties suit products you already sell with a paid warranty. Test whether a temporary free warranty attracts the audience that has been sitting on your list. [L1204.030]

#### Free white glove as a monetary promotion

- Free white glove in-home delivery can be used as a monetary promotion in the same way as free expedited shipping. It can be restricted to certain suppliers or to orders above a certain price. [L1205.009]

#### Product page banner template

Place the banner directly on the product page where the offer applies. (older, 2022)

```
Limited time: free white glove in-home delivery on purchases of [minimum amount, e.g., $1,500] or more. No code necessary. Includes bringing the product into your home, setting it up, and removing all boxes and packaging.
```

[L1281.042]

#### Assembly and installation

- Clearly state assembly requirements, shipping format, and customer responsibility on product pages. Consider offering assembly as a paid upsell by partnering with assembly services used by competitors. (older, 2021) [L0270.047]
- For products requiring assembly or installation, research what your competitors use for assembly services. Partner with the same service provider. Offer assembly as a paid upsell on the product page so customers can add professional installation if desired. (older, 2021) [L0270.068]

### Optional services and add-ons

- For freight shipments, offer optional services such as liftgate, inside delivery, and white glove service, and mark them up. Check with suppliers for any other custom options you can also charge for. [L1357.024]
- Item customizations such as custom colors can be marked up and work well for high-ticket items. Some customers send a hex code, which suppliers can often match. [L1357.025]
- If the manufacturer charges $100 for inside delivery, add a markup such as 10% to the customer's inside delivery line and record the base cost in the card notes. This keeps the owner's cost visible and the margin clear. Use the same markup rule for every quote of that type. (Source B, older, 2021-22) [L1396.023]
- For an everyday free-shipping offer, do not add it as a line item in the cart. That creates a draft line item that blocks discount codes. Name the $0 shipping option instead, for example "free liftgate delivery." (older, 2022) [L0963.069]
- Free shipping and paid add-ons: Shopify's shipping system and product options together can handle upgrades only as separate options, since free and paid shipping cannot both be offered on the same shipping setup (older, 2021). [L0247.035]

### Promotions that include shipping

- Free shipping can be used when margin covers it. For non-MAP products with high shipping costs, raise the product price to include shipping and show free shipping. [L0941.053]
- Free shipping is offered as a limited-time promotion, or through a discount code, as described in the free-shipping thresholds section. [L0502.015]
- Free expedited shipping, free white glove in-home delivery, and free liftgate are $0 options that can be used as monetary promotions, restricted to chosen suppliers or to orders above a set price. [L1205.008, L1205.009, L0963.069]

### Merchant Center consistency

- When implementing shipping fees in Shopify, update the corresponding settings in Google Merchant Center with the same rules. Discrepancies between the two are flagged by Google as quality issues. (older, 2022) [L0249.039]
- Shipping costs in Google Shopping ads are shown beside competitor prices, so the Merchant Center shipping settings must match the store's live shipping rules. [L0249.039, L0966.051]
- Where a heavy item uses a separate shipping profile (for example, to remain competitive on Google Shopping), that profile must be reflected in Merchant Center. [L0470.031, L0249.039]

#### Further details

- Use geo-targeting in paid ads to limit to regions near the supplier warehouse if shipping costs are high. This keeps shipping expenses lower and preserves profit margins. [L0249.036]

- When you offer free shipping, the customer is not charged for shipping but you pay it, and that cost comes out of your own margin. The supplier only gives free shipping if they choose to, so it is not automatic. [L1480.019]

## Bonuses and free gifts

### Types of bonuses

- Bonus offers have four types: physical products (low-cost items customers need), digital products (online lessons, guides, e-books), expedited shipping upgrades (white-glove delivery), and extended warranties. [L0314.001]
- Physical product bonuses have historically given a stronger conversion lift than digital bonuses. Digital bonuses are covered under the digital versus physical comparison below. [L0314.001]
- A bonus is a free item tied to a purchase trigger, such as spending above a threshold or buying a specific product. Physical bonuses are shipped goods, and digital bonuses are delivered online. [L0757.022]
- Bonus items can include free ebooks, video guides, styling tutorials, complimentary gifts, or storage boxes. They increase perceived value at minimal cost and make rejection emotionally difficult for customers. [L0233.009]
- Bonuses such as ebooks, video tutorials on how the product works, or videos on how it improves the buyer's life increase the value of an offer. Add them to the landing page as part of the offer. [L0502.011]
- Free gifts, free upsells, installation services, and valuable content such as recipe books add perceived value. Consistently helpful free content throughout the buyer journey can build a higher perceived value than competitors. [L0411.031] *(Source B, 2021-22: older advice.)*
- Free gift card: a gift card can serve as the free gift when a store wants to add value to an order and encourage a larger purchase. It works as an upsell because the buyer gets extra value without a physical product from the supplier. Show the gift clearly on the offer image so the visitor sees what they get. [L1363.059]
- Premium with its own value: add a premium that carries its own value, such as a one-year magazine subscription included with a set, so the same payment looks like more value. Name the premium's price or worth in the letter. [L1369.016]
- Gift with purchase from another range: buying one product gives a free second product from a different range, typically a smaller, high-margin item that adds little weight, so shipping cost stays low. [L1404.019]
- Services the business already provides can be presented as bonuses at no extra cost to the seller. [L0683.009]
- White-glove delivery means the delivery company brings the product inside, unpacks it, removes all packaging, and positions it. It costs an extra $100-$150 per order. [L0314.012]
- Physical bonuses should be items the buyer would need anyway, such as a seasonal ingredient pack, a protectant for leather furniture, or a book drop-shipped with the order. [L1204.039]

### Deciding whether a bonus pays

- Bonus offers must be strategised before traffic launch so they are built into the sales funnel as a proven conversion multiplier. [L0040.015]
- Offer free gifts or bonuses only when the margin allows it, such as on a best-selling product with strong profit per sale in a competitive niche. Free gifts make sense when competitors do not offer them and the product margin covers the cost. [L0941.052]
- Example: a free bonus gift worth about $50 can make sense on a product that earns roughly $400 to $500 profit per sale in a competitive niche, because competitors do not offer it and the gift drives extra orders. If the margin on the product is thin, do not offer a gift. [L0941.108]
- Include free gifts only when the margin still works after the gift cost. If a supplier charges state tax on shipments and this cannot be resolved, drop the free gifts. [L0968.078] *(Source A, 2021: older advice.)*
- Keep the gift's cost low enough that it does not eat into the margin, and skew the value by setting a higher price point for the bundle. [L0999.065]
- Bundling a low-cost item with the main product, or adding a free gift that costs little, can raise perceived value and average order value while keeping margin healthy, as long as the gift is relevant and the customer wants it. [L0999.064]
- The bonus strategy is worth more when product margin is high. On a $300 or $800 profit product, a $20 bonus is negligible and can be given freely. [L1293.026]
- Very low-priced add-ons whose shipping cost is close to their price should not be sold alone. Offer them as a free bonus gift with high-ticket purchases, or sell them in multi-packs. [L0966.054] *(Source A, 2025.)*
- Do not sell low-ticket accessories on their own. If the margin allows, include them as bonus gifts with a main product instead. [L0968.112] *(Source A, 2021: older advice.)*
- Decide between a free bonus and a cross-sell by comparing the main product price with the price of the add-on. [L1110.028] *(Source A, 2019: older advice.)*
- Cheap accessories on an expensive product should be listed as free bonuses on the product page, without their own product pages. Example: a 5,000 USD bike with a reflector and water bottle holder costing about 30 USD retail and 15 USD to the seller. [L1110.028] *(Source A, 2019: older advice.)*
- Gift bonuses reduce the need for price cuts: a free accessory that costs the store $25 but is valued at $200 can be given with every order. [L1109.031] *(Source A, 2018: older advice.)*
- Competitor stores add a free bonus item such as a guide ebook to a bundle. Use a bonus to raise perceived value without changing the base price. [L0600.021]

### Choosing gifts by cost and relevance

- Pick bonus items that cost little for you but solve buyer problems, such as storage, styling, cleaning, or wearing advice. Low-cost items like a jewelry box or an AI-drafted ebook can carry a large share of the perceived value. [L0502.016]
- Bonus items should be inexpensive for you to offer, even if they look valuable to the buyer. Do not choose items with high wholesale costs or ones that threaten your margins, because bonuses are incentives for customers to choose you, not revenue-builders. [L0314.015]
- Bonus items that customers would need anyway with the product are the best choice. Ideally the bonus comes from the same manufacturer, so it can be added to the supplier's order at no extra effort. [L1293.027]
- Physical bonus pairings that work: a book for spending over $50; free ingredients or free bottles with a starter kit; a free protectant with a leather sofa. [L0757.023]
- Physical bonus ideas: a homebrewing starter kit can include a free ingredient sample pack or free bottles if bought before a deadline. Leather sofas can include a free leather protectant shipped with every order during a few promotion days each month. [L1205.015]
- The bonus should be closely related to the product's use or benefit. An unrelated free ebook is weak. For example, a shower filter should get an ebook on skin care for sensitive skin, linked to hard-water skin problems. [L0577.016]
- A free gift that is unrelated to the main product but valued by buyers can make an offer irresistible and help differentiate the store from competitors. Pick a low-cost gift that buyers want, such as a shaker or water bottle. [L1173.022]
- Test whether the gift matches what the target buyer wants. Free packaging or free shipping insurance can be offered as a free-gift tab on some themes, but these are not always what buyers value. [L1173.024]
- Choose a gift item that matches what the buyer needs, such as a cleaning product for a sofa. [L0963.068]
- Choose a gift the buyer will value, such as a popular consumer electronic item unrelated to the store's products, and check that its cost fits the margin on the main sale. [L1223.032]
- Bonuses should be designed specifically for the main offer, not thrown together. Each one should be valuable enough that the prospect would pay for it alone. Start by asking which bonuses would perfectly complement the main product and add enormous value to the offer. [L1365.037]
- Choose a promotion that feels valuable to customers and is still profitable. A free gift with high perceived value often moves more customers than a slightly larger money-off discount. [L1112.068] *(Source A, 2021: older advice.)*
- A bonus gift with a perceived value of about $100, such as a leather desk mat, can persuade buyers to buy from your store instead of the supplier. Its cost to you is only the wholesale price of the gift, so it works best when the supplier does not offer the same extra. [L0934.003] *(Source A, 2023.)*
- Choose bonus items from the same supplier as the main product to avoid extra shipping costs. Bonus items from different suppliers arrive separately and add shipping. [L0962.106] *(Source A, 2021: older advice.)*
- Add-ons do not need to come from the same supplier as the main product. Choose the add-on that is cheapest to buy and has the highest perceived value for the customer. [L0577.012]
- Add-on pricing: the add-on's real cost is irrelevant to the buyer. Only the stated value matters, so label it with a clearly higher value. [L0577.015]
- Cheap free gifts such as a $1 item from AliExpress can be used as a bonus to entice buyers, as long as the business keeps a profit margin. [L0650.042]
- Adding a complementary free item, for example socks with hoodies, or a free digital ebook raises perceived value and allows a higher offer price, as long as the item is cheap to produce or obtain. [L0650.040]
- A free digital bonus, such as an ebook, can be added to every order as a zero-cost extra that makes the purchase more attractive. It works when the product margin is too thin for a bigger discount. [L0562.041]
- If a margin is too thin for a $20 gift, give something that costs nothing, such as a digital recipe e-book, a video tutorial, or a free online training course, when it adds value for the customer. [L1293.030]
- Educational bonuses such as ebooks raise the perceived value of an offer, especially in health and wellness, kids, and parenting niches. This can allow a slightly higher offer price while still giving the customer a feeling of extra value. [L0650.069]
- Example: a necklace offer gave the buyer one main product plus a second necklace of their choice for free. Most buyers chose a design linked to their home country. The offer raised perceived value while keeping the free item's cost low. [L0661.027]
- Physical bonuses should be items the buyer would need anyway. Free books were also used as a bonus on orders over $50 in one store. [L1204.039]
- Two other examples of bonus items that carry perceived value: a free trending product offered with any order before a deadline, and a bonus of a snorkel with a mask. [L1111.090, L1293.028]
- Bonus pairings that match the main product's use: a free snorkel with a scuba mask, a free carrying case with a camera lens, a free air pump with a $300 air mattress (a $30 to $40 pump, since no air mattress comes with one and customers must buy it separately). [L1293.025, L1293.028, L1293.029]
- When a supplier includes a bonus item such as a bag or accessory, show it prominently as a "Free gift" block with a picture and its value. This makes the included extra visible and increases the perceived value of the offer. [L1130.030]
- Free accessories bundled with a main product are generally acceptable if the accessories are also sold separately at MAP. Check each supplier's policy if in doubt. [L1069.054] *(Source A, 2019: older advice.)*
- Bundle low-cost extras as free bonus gifts rather than raising the base price, so the advertised price stays the same and MAP is not violated. [L1109.030] *(Source A, 2018: older advice.)*

### Digital bonuses and free ebooks

- Digital bonus ideas: an e-book version of a guide given free for orders over $50; a free lesson, e-book, or downloadable video that matches what the customer needs; or a free extended trial of a related service through an affiliate partnership, such as a treadmill store offering a weight-management program trial. [L1205.016]
- Digital bonus pairings: spend over $50 for a free eBook; buy a drum set for free online lessons; buy a treadmill for an extended free trial of a weight-loss program such as Weight Watchers. [L0757.023]
- Digital bonuses (online lessons, guides, e-books) underperform physical bonuses in conversion lift, based on practical experience. When using digital bonuses, partner with external instructors or platforms rather than creating your own content. This reduces cost and leverages their expertise. [L0314.009]
- Example: a drum-set seller partnered with an online drum lesson platform to offer one or two free introductory lessons. The platform gains new customers, the store differentiates, and the bonus has minimal or no cost to the store. [L0314.010]
- Example: treadmill sellers can offer physical bonuses such as a free water bottle, towels, or apparel, or digital bonuses such as a free meal plan or an e-book on running technique. Test which types customers value most. [L0314.011]
- Educational bonuses should be checked against the product: the best bonus is directly linked to the buyer's use or benefit. [L0577.016]
- A free ebook worth $20 with a purchase increases conversion and can be delivered automatically after the order. [L1184.048]
- A digital bonus such as an ebook was tested as extra value but did not beat a relevant physical free gift in one store. Prefer a physical gift that is closely related to the main product and its customer problem. [L1315.062]
- Free digital add-ons such as guides or templates can be listed at a stated value and given free with purchase, which makes the gift feel worth more. Customers who missed the add-on earlier can be offered it free later as a reason to buy again. [L0774.055]
- Free digital gift pattern: add a simple free guide, for example one made with ChatGPT and a couple of images, to the order to add value without cost. Some customers may pay a small price for the guide, after which the offer can be given free to previous buyers. [L0773.055]
- A free digital ebook that costs nothing to create can justify a $5.99 higher price for a bigger bundle tier, increasing perceived value at no cost. [L0232.008]

### Creating free ebooks

#### Scope and content

- Keep the ebook short: 15-25 pages with 4-6 chapters of substantive, useful content. Each chapter must deliver real value, and the ebook must contain no placeholder text. [L0860.011]
- Ebooks cost almost nothing to deliver but noticeably raise perceived value. Customers feel they are getting expert information, and the offer feels more complete even if they never read the ebook. [L0860.012]
- Page count guidance: a free ebook should have around 15 to 30 pages to deliver perceived value, while 5 pages is too few. Show the page count on the landing page, for example "a 28-page PDF." [L1439.050]
- Choose an ebook topic that matches the product's niche and the buyer's problem. A dog product gets a dog health guide, a fitness product gets a home workout plan, and a sleep product gets a sleep guide. [L0860.013]
