- Generic bundle names fail to create perceived value. [L0232.022]
- Repackaging a product into a higher-priced bundle SKU lowers click-through rate. [L1109.032] (older advice, Source A 2018)
- Buy-one-get-one-free can downgrade a premium or identity-based brand. [L1131.012]
- A second identical item has little value to a buyer who only needs one. [L1131.013]
- Bundling does not work when the store offers only one product in BOGO form (for example one book), so use Buy 1, Buy 2 and Buy 3 quantity tiers with progressive discounts instead. [L0860.005, L1149.006]
- Buy-one-get-one can be a barrier for single-gift buyers. [L1225.015]
- Turning single items around $50 into bundles only to reach a higher price is not advised. [L0804.088] (Source A 2023)
- Bundles can lose money if the bundle price does not cover product cost, acquisition cost and fees. [L0436.025, L0233.024]

### Build-your-own and offers that look like free items

- Build-your-own bundle: up to 15% off, free shipping, and any combination of products the customer chooses. [L0524.025]
- Show the add-on as almost free: list the complementary item at a visible value (e.g. $20) while its cost is about $2, and show a bundle discount so the add-on looks almost free. [L1260.022]

### Pricing for Google Shopping and bundle take-rate

- Bundle take-rate and profit: complementary bundles with a 40 to 50 percent take rate and about $40 extra profit per order (see the complementary section). [L0650.038]
- Bundling three related items into one set is a way to raise perceived value and average order size. [L1153.030]

### Bundle types

- **Complementary add-on bundles:** a low-cost accessory is paired with a main product and shown as one offer. [L1345.024]
- **Quantity bundles (one, two and three packs):** each larger pack gives more savings per unit. These are a core way to raise order value, alongside volume discounts and cart upsells. [L1410.003]
- **Buy-one-get-one and free-item bundles:** a second unit or a second item is offered free with the first. [L1404.018, L1436.043, L1439.034]
- **On-page bundle packages:** a group of products tied to a core product, sold at one combined price. [L1409.010]
- **Value bundles:** one item is discounted to roughly break-even and profit comes from the other bundled items. [L1522.017]
- Bundles are often combined with percentage-off or dollar-off discounts. [L1404.023]

### Complementary add-ons

- Example: a complementary item costing about $2 is listed at $20. Bundled with the main product, the bundle shows a $15 discount on the add-on, so the buyer perceives the add-on as $5. [L1345.024]
- A bundle discount of about $15 on a $20 complementary item is considered an attractive discount level. Set the actual discount according to margin. [L1345.025]
- Create bundles for popular add-ons and accessories as separate Shopify products. This gives more listings in Google Shopping, where the single product and several bundles can appear together for one search. [L1357.021]
- Start with bundles for the best-selling accessories and test them before adding more. Bundles for every combination create too many options and overwhelm customers. The best approach is an educated guess followed by testing. [L1357.022]
- Bundling only works where the product is naturally bought with something else. It is not possible for products never sold alongside another item. Where it works, a bundle can add roughly one or two hundred dollars of profit per sale. [L1456.015]

### Quantity-break ladders

#### Tier structure and pricing

Example ladder from a drop-shipping brand (USD):

| Quantity | Price |
|---|---|
| 1 pack | 39 |
| 2 packs | 59 |
| 3 packs | 79 |

[L1410.004, L1412.007]

- The source labels the 2-pack deal as saving 35. Two separate packs cost 78, so the real saving is 19 (78 − 59). Check the label against the actual prices before showing it. [L1410.004, L1412.007]
- Derived from the same table: three packs at 79 save 38 against three single packs at 39 (117 − 79). This figure is not stated in the source. [L1412.007]
- Each extra unit is priced with a steeper discount so the bigger order looks clearly better. In a separate example, a 2-pack saved the buyer $35 and a 3-pack saved $62 at the time of that example. These figures do not match the ladder above, so recalculate them from the prices you actually use. [L1411.005]
- Multi-pack offers can keep margin healthy even with a discount. When the same product ships to the same address, product and shipping cost per unit fall. Buyers also gain value and may gift the extra units. [L1411.004]
- Volume bundles can be varied by pack count or by family size. The bundle should show the total price tiers clearly. Shown together, the tiers give the customer a reason to add more units. [L1412.009]

#### Which products get pack ladders

- For products priced under $50, a one-pack / two-pack bundle should always be implemented. For expensive products a bundle can still work, and the pack type can be changed to family packs. [L1410.005]
- For products priced under 50 USD, a bundle offer such as one pack, two packs or a family pack is recommended. Expensive products can also use bundles, but the strongest case is low-priced items. The two sources differ in strength: "should always" for under $50 in one, "recommended" in the other. Use the pack ladder for low-priced products first. [L1410.005, L1412.008]

### Tier naming and badges

- Name a bundle tier, such as Gold, by using the same product name plus a suffix that makes it sound like a better deal. [L1456.014]
- Multi-pack tiers with a "Best Value" tag and a visible savings amount raise the average order size. [L1479.023]
- Example from a 2025 US supplement page: a single bottle at $24.99, a "Buy Two Get 1 Free" multi-pack, a larger multi-pack tagged "Best Value" with a "You Save" amount shown, and an automatic refill option that saves 20%. [L1479.022]
- Pairing multi-pack tiers with an automatic refill discount, such as 20% off, adds repeat revenue from the same customer. [L1479.023]

### Buy-one-get-one and free-item offers

- Multi-purchase deals such as 2 for 1 or buy 1 get 1 free make the customer feel they get a great deal, which raises the chance of buying. This format has worked for years and still works today. [L1399.010]
- Multi-purchase deals such as buy 2 for 1 or buy 1 get 1 free make shoppers feel they are getting a strong deal, which raises the chance of buying. This is an old promotion that still works well today. [L1400.007]
- BOGO works best on the same product as the one purchased, because customers who want that item already have an emotional attachment to it. A variant such as Buy One Get One at 30% off is also possible. [L1404.018]
- A buy-one-get-one-free offer works well for the same product. It also works for very simple products that are understood from one image. For a different or complex product, the offer lowers conversion because the buyer must check the landing page. [L1412.040]
- Buy-one-get-one-free and "buy this, get this free item" bundles have been working for TikTok dropshipping. Buy-one-get-one may fail with electronics, while jewelry may suit it. [L1436.043]
- Free-item example: buy one piece and receive a second piece for free. Customers who tagged the brand on Instagram also received a 50 discount (the source gives no unit) and a feature on the brand page. [L1439.034]

### Cart-page quantity ladders

- On the cart page, a quantity ladder can reward larger baskets: one unit gets no discount and two units get 10% off. This gives a buyer already in checkout a simple reason to add a second unit. [L1412.055]

### Older advice (2021-22): on-page bundles and value bundles

- Example bundle on a product page (older, 2021-22):

| Item | Regular price (USD) | Bundle price (USD) |
|---|---|---|
| Golf simulator package | 7,799 | 7,299 |
| Tech bundle | not given | 1,899 |
| Projector floor mount enclosure | not given | 299 |
| Turf landing pad | 499 | 399 |
| Total | 10,496 | 9,896 |

The bundled total is $9,896 instead of $10,496 when bought separately. The $10,496 separate total equals 7,799 + 1,899 + 299 + 499. [L1409.009]

- On-page bundle boxes work better with several products tied to a core product, but a single product can also be offered this way. The customer sees a package with a combined price that is lower than the individual prices. [L1409.010]
- A value bundle groups products so one item is discounted to roughly break-even while profit comes from the bundled items. The bundle total must look cheaper than buying the items separately, and the page should explain the added value. [L1522.017]

#### Protecting margin on a low-margin product (older, 2021-22)

1. Find a supporting product or accessory that customers buy alongside it. [L1456.014]
2. Create a named bundle tier, such as Gold, using the same product name plus a suffix that makes it sound like a better deal. [L1456.014]
3. Add the bundle as a selectable option on the product page, changing the price, or as its own product page that appears in ads. [L1456.014]
4. Use the same search traffic for both versions. Even selling the bundle to only part of buyers can recover a meaningful part of the margin lost. [L1456.014]

### Bundle strategy by price point and product type

- Start with the best-selling accessories and test them. [L1357.022]
- Under $50: use one-pack and two-pack bundles first. [L1410.005, L1412.008]
- Expensive products: bundles can still work, with family packs as an option. [L1410.005]
- Simple products understood from one image suit buy-one-get-one offers. Complex products lower conversion with those offers. [L1412.040]
- Electronics may fail with buy-one-get-one offers, while jewelry may suit them. [L1436.043]
- Bundles only work for products naturally bought together. [L1456.015]

### Free shipping and gifts

- On multi-pack orders to the same address, shipping cost per unit falls, which helps keep margin healthy under a discount. Buyers may gift the extra units. [L1411.004]
- A free second item can be attached to a purchase, and social actions can unlock extra rewards. [L1439.034]

#### Further details

- A bundle offer on two units raises average order value. Use a simple discount code for single purchases to keep the entry offer easy to see. [L0492.035]

- Progressive quantity discounts, with a larger discount for more units, raise the average order value and make the bundle feel like the better deal compared with buying one unit. [L1149.007]

## Building bundles in Shopify and apps

### Choose the offer type before you build

- Bundle offers outperform Monster Card for average order value. Monster Card and bundle tactics are incompatible and should not run at the same time. [L0298.033]
- Bundle apps work better for increasing the quantity of the same product. Monster Card works better when you have different complementary items to cross-sell. Choose by your specific goal. [L0298.056]
- Use an automated discount rather than a discount code for a buy-X-get-Y offer. The discount applies in the cart without the customer entering anything, and the offer stays identical at every step from the ad to checkout. [L0650.066]
- Bundle builders with quantity discounts usually need a paid app. Built-in bundle features in a theme can replace several monthly app costs. [L0709.021]
- One bundle app was estimated to cost about as much per month as the full $249 theme price over roughly six months. Compare total app costs with a one-time theme price to judge the savings. [L0709.022]
- Common conversion features are bundle builders, quizzes, upsells, cross-sells, free shipping options, and discounts for adding more items. They were built into a theme because they were seen converting well across many brand projects. [L0709.023]

### Shopify automatic discounts (no bundle app needed)

- Shopify automatic discount for a quantity rule: go to Discounts, click Create discount, choose Automatic discount, and set a rule such as "buy 2 and get 20% off". Show a banner message on the product page or in the cart. Example: 2 products at 34.99 USD each (69.99 USD) become 59.99 USD at checkout. Apps such as Bundle Bear are optional and not needed at the start. [L0773.017]
- Source check: 20% off 69.99 USD is 55.99 USD, not 59.99 USD. The example shows about 14% off. Confirm the discount value in the Shopify rule before you publish the banner. [L0773.017]
- Set up the automatic quantity discount in Shopify: Discounts > Create discount > automatic discount. Communicate the offer on the product page, for example "20% off when two items are added to the cart". This works without a bundle or upsell app. [L0774.014]
- Create an automatic product discount for a bundle:
  1. Go to Discounts and create a new discount.
  2. Select Amount off products.
  3. Set the method to Automatic.
  4. Enter a Title. Customers see this title in the cart and at checkout.
  5. Set Discount value to Percentage and enter the value (for example 10%).
  6. Under Applies to, choose Specific collections and select the collection that holds all bundle items.
  7. Set a minimum purchase requirement of 4 items so the discount applies only to a full bundle.
  8. Decide whether the discount may combine with other discounts.
  9. Set the start date under Active dates and, if needed, an end date.
  10. Save, then check the Performance section for usage. [L0559.015]
- The automatic discount summary should read Amount off products, Method Automatic and Details For Online Store, with the percentage and minimum item count listed. The discount must be active before testing, otherwise the cart will not show it. [L0559.016]
- Set the Shopify minimum purchase quantity to the number of items in the bundle, so the discount triggers only when the full bundle is in the cart. A minimum below the bundle size gives the discount on partial sets. [L0559.018]
- Formula: Bundle price after discount = list total x (1 - discount %). Example: a list total of $37.00 with a 10% discount gives $37.00 x (1 - 0.10) = $33.30, a saving of $3.70. [L0559.019]
- If the widget discount percentage and the Shopify discount value differ, the shopper sees one saving on the bundle and gets another at checkout. Keep both values identical for each bundle. [L0559.022]
- In Shopify, a quantity-based discount can be set to apply when two items are in the cart, so the customer sees the saving immediately. The lesson's amount is 10, but its unit is not stated. Confirm whether it is a percentage or a fixed amount in your store before publishing. [L0436.017]

### Bundle Bear

**Tool description and offer types**

- Bundle Bear is a Shopify app for volume discount offers, such as buy two and get 40% off. [L1259.028, L1260.021]
- In Bundle Bear, the Bundle and Volume discount offer types are the most practical choices for most stores. A bundle can offer a fixed saving on two complementary products, or make the second product a free gift with the first. [L0634.040]
- Bundle Bear can combine two different products into one deal, such as a cap with a free t-shirt, and can run buy-one-get-one-free offers. Each bundle can use the brand color and brand images, and its performance can be checked in the analytics. [L0635.034]
- Bundle Bear offer types: bundle with another product, volume discount (the more bought, the less paid), mix and match (discount for different products), and cart goal (discount above a cart value such as $50). [L1297.033]

**Setup procedures**

- Create a bundle offer in Bundle Bear:
  1. Install Bundle Bear from the Shopify App Store and click Install app.
  2. Click Create offer and choose Bundle.
  3. Select the first and second product.
  4. Choose the saving. Options: a fixed amount off for both items (for example $10 or $20), the second product as a free gift, or no discount, to show that the products are sold together.
  5. Under advanced settings, set a start and end date for urgency and round prices, choosing 0.99 as the ending.
  6. Click save. [L0634.026]
- Create a volume discount in Bundle Bear:
  1. Install Bundle Bear from the Shopify App Store.
  2. Click Create an offer and select Volume discount.
  3. Select the specific product and search for it.
  4. Add tiers, for example Buy 1 and save 10%, Buy 2 and save 15%, Buy 3 and save 20%, and customize the text for each tier.
  5. Save. The offer appears just below the product.
  
  You can also set brand color and images, create buy-one-get-one or free-gift offers, and check bundle analytics. [L0635.025]
- Create a Bundle Bear volume discount (second procedure):
  1. Install Bundle Bear from the Shopify App Store and click Install app.
  2. Create an offer and name it, for example "offer one".
  3. Choose the offer type: bundle with another product, volume discount, mix and match, or cart goal.
  4. For this example choose volume discount, then pick all products or specific products.
  5. Before setting the discount, calculate the fulfillment cost per unit with your supplier.
  6. Add the discount tiers, then click save and activate.
  7. In the theme editor, add the Bundle Bear block to the product page, usually below the quantity selector. [L1297.033]
- Bundle Bear button label: a bundle offer can use a label such as "Get my deal" instead of "Add to cart" or "Buy now". The label and savings text can be edited in the bundle app. [L1410.043]

**Tier values and examples**

- Typical volume-discount tiers are buy 2 for 10% off and buy 3 for 15% off. A cheaper alternative to Bundle Bear is the Sketching Bundle app. [L0635.026]
- Tier values differ between sources. L0635.025 uses Buy 1 save 10%, Buy 2 save 15%, Buy 3 save 20%. L1297.034 uses 10% for 2 items, 15% for 3 items and 20% for 4 items. Both are examples. Calculate your fulfillment cost per unit before you choose the tiers. [L0635.025, L0635.026, L1297.034, L1297.033]
- Worked example: 10% off for 2 items, 15% off for 3 items and 20% off for 4 items. Two items were priced at about $201 instead of about $223, so one item cost about $100 instead of about $112. [L1297.034]

**Advanced settings and rounding**

- In Bundle Bear advanced settings, you can add custom badges, set a start and end time for urgency, and round discounted prices to 50 cents or $1. The example rounded prices to 50 cents. [L1297.035]
- For Bundle Bear discounted prices, enable rounding and choose the.99 ending, for example to show a bundle price as 49.99 instead of 50.00. Set this in the advanced settings of the offer. [L0476.030]
- Rounding values differ between sources. Use the.99 ending for charm pricing, as in L0476.030 and L0634.026. Use 50 cents or $1 when you want whole-amount rounding, as in L1297.035, where the example used 50 cents. [L0476.030, L0634.026, L1297.035]

### Kaching Bundles

**Setup procedures**

- Set up a quantity-break bundle in Kaching Bundles (single-product example):
  1. Search "Kaching Bundles" in the Shopify App Store, install it, and approve the billing permissions.
  2. Open the app and create a new bundle deal.
  3. Select the product to bundle (one product, here a single supplement).
  4. Enter a name for the bundle (only visible to you).
  5. Set the Block title to a free-shipping threshold above the one-item price and between the one-item and two-item totals (example: "Free Shipping for orders above $25" for a $19.95 product).
  6. Set the Discount name shown in cart and checkout to "Bundle & Save".
  7. Leave all other settings at default except Price rounding.
  8. Enable Price rounding and choose 95 or 99 cents.
  9. If the product has no variants, turn off "Let customers choose different variants for each item".
  10. Bar #1 (Buy One): set quantity to 1 and keep Full price. Title "Buy One". Delete the subtitle. In the price line, insert the variable for new price per item followed by "/ bottle". In the label, type "SAVE" followed by the variable for saved total. Badge style "Simple", badge text "Great Start!".
  11. Bar #2 (Buy Two): set quantity to 2 and choose Fixed price with a total of 29.99. Title "Buy Two". Subtitle and label identical to each other. Badge style "Most Popular". Optionally select it by default.
  12. Bar #3 (Buy Three): set quantity to 3 and choose Fixed price with a total of 38.95. Title "Buy Three". Badge text "Best Value".
  13. Add a free e-book gift to Bar #2 and Bar #3 (and to Bar #4 if you add one).
  14. Open Style and change colors, fonts and corner radius to your brand.
  15. Click Save as draft, check the preview, then click Publish. [L0862.001]
- Set up a bundle deal in Kaching Bundles (generic version):
  1. Search Kaching Bundles in the Shopify App Store, install it and approve the billing permissions.
  2. Open the app, go to Bundle deals, then click Create bundle deal.
  3. Under the product selection, choose the product that the bundle is built around.
  4. In Settings, enter a name (visible only to you), a block title that carries the free shipping message (for example "Free shipping for orders above $25") and the discount name shown in cart and checkout (for example "Bundle & Save").
  5. Keep all other settings at default except Price rounding.
  6. Choose the deal type. Quantity breaks for the same product is the usual default.
  7. Bar #1 Buy One: set Quantity to 1, Full price, title "Buy One", delete the subtitle, Badge style "Simple", badge text "Great Start!", label "SAVE" with the saved total variable, and the price line with the new price per item followed by "/Bottle".
  8. Bar #2 Buy Two: set Quantity to 2, Fixed price with a total of 29.99, title "Buy Two", subtitle and label identical to the title, Badge style "Most Popular", optionally "Selected by default".
  9. Bar #3 Buy Three: set Quantity to 3, Fixed price with the chosen total, title "Buy Three", Badge style "Best Value".
  10. Add a free gift to bars 2 and 3: Add gift, Single gift, rename it "Free e-book" and select the e-book product.
  11. In Settings, turn off the variant selector if the product has no variants.
  12. In Style, change colors, font sizes and corner radius to the brand.
  13. Use Preview to check the product and the country (for example United States) and confirm the prices display correctly.
  14. Click Save as draft, then Publish. A Setup A/B test option is available before publishing. [L0861.003]

**Deal types and templates**

- Bundle deal types available in Kaching Bundles:

| Type | Layout | Example labels in the app |
|---|---|---|
| Quantity breaks, same product | 1 / 2 / 3 packs of one product | Complete the bundle, Save $10.00!, Buy 2 |
| Quantity breaks, different products | 1 / 2 / 3 packs of different products | SAVE 15% |
| Buy X, Get Y (BXGY) | Buy 1 get 1 free, Buy 2 get 3 free, Buy 3 get 6 free | Buy 1, get 1 free SAVE 60%; Buy 2, get 3 free SAVE 68% |
| Subscription | Subscribe and save | Subscribe & Save 20% |
| Progressive gifts | Unlock free gifts as the order grows | Free shipping, FREE gift, Locked |

[L0861.015]

- Quantity breaks are the usual choice for most products. The other deal types are alternatives for specific products, such as subscription or BXGY offers. Pick the type that matches how the customer will buy the product. [L0861.016]
- For supplements, the subscription template is a good fit. For most other products, quantity breaks are good enough. Any bar can be customized, so quantity breaks for 1, 2 and 3 units (optionally 4) is a sound default. [L0862.008]
- Available bundle templates in Kaching Bundles: Quantity breaks for the same product, Quantity breaks for different products, Buy X Get Y deals (for example Buy 1 get 1 free, Buy 2 get 3 free, Buy 3 get 6 free), Subscription & Save with 20% off, Progressive gifts that unlock free gifts with an order, a Volume discount with other products, countdown timer and scratch-off styles. [L0862.007]
- The deal creator asks you to choose a discount type from Single or Duo templates, with presets such as "You save 15%", "Buy 1, get 1 free" and "Buy 3, get 6 free". Any template can be changed afterwards. Pick the closest preset to speed up setup, then edit the prices and labels. [L0861.040]
- Create a highlighted offer: in the Kaching Bundles app, create a bundle offer, add quantity breaks to it, and mark the best-selling offer as highlighted so customers see it first. [L1324.007]

**Pricing and fixed totals**

- Use Fixed price for bundle bars instead of percentage discounts. You can type the exact total, and the per-unit price is then easy to show. Example: a fixed total of 29.99 for two bottles displays as about 14.98 per bottle. [L0862.002]
- Source check: 29.99 divided by 2 is 14.995, so the exact per-bottle figure is about 14.995 and the source displays 14.98. Use the price shown in your Preview. [L0862.002]
- Reference bundle prices from the example, for a $19.95 single bottle:
  - Buy Two at a fixed total of $29.99 (about $14.98 per bottle, compare-at $40.00).
  - Buy Three at a fixed total of $38.95 (about $12.98 per bottle, compare-at $60.00).
  - Price rounding set to 95 shows prices ending in.95. [L0862.003]
- Formula for the SAVE label: Saved amount = compare-at price of the bundle minus the bundle total. Example: a compare-at price of $40.00 and a bundle total of $29.95 shows SAVE $10.05. [L0861.012]
- Pricing settings: in Settings > Pricing, the toggle "Update theme product price" sits next to Price rounding and the other price display options. Turn it on only if the theme should show the bundle-adjusted price on the product page. Confirm the result in Preview before publishing. [L0861.037]

**Variables for labels and price lines**

- The bracket button in Kaching Bundles label and price fields opens an "Add variable" menu with preset maths. The variables are: New unit price, Saved $ per unit, Original unit price, Unit quantity, Buy quantity, Got quantity, Discount, Saved percentage, Saved $ total, Product title, New total price, New price per item and Original total price.
- Use New price per item with the unit label for the bar price line, and Saved $ total in the SAVE label. [L0861.011, L0862.036]

**Bars and badges**

- Bar settings: Add upsell, Add gift, Add personalisation, Add highlights, Show as Sold out, Show quantity selector and Selected by default. Each bar can carry its own title, subtitle, label, badge style and badge text. [L0861.017]
- Badge styles in the example bundle: Simple for the single-unit bar, Most Popular for the two-unit bar and Best Value for the three-unit bar. Badge text is free to set, for example "Great Start!", "Most Popular" and "Best Value". [L0861.018]
- Bundle with a free gift: a free gift on the two-unit and three-unit bars gives the customer both a discount and extra value for buying more. This helps move them from one unit to at least two. Use the same gift logic on any higher-quantity bar. [L0861.020]

**Gifts**

- Gifts added to a bundle receive an automatic 100% discount, so the free item costs nothing at checkout. Add the free gift to the Buy Two and Buy Three bars, and to a Buy Four bar if one exists. A buy-four bar needs its own gift. [L0861.019, L0862.005]
- Gift options in a bar: Add gift offers "Single gift" or "Multiple gifts selector". Toggles: Show original price, Include in compare-at price and Apply only for subscriptions.
  - Leave "Include in compare-at price" on if the free gift's value should count toward the savings shown.
  - Use "Apply only for subscriptions" to limit a gift to subscription orders. [L0861.039, L0862.033]

**Variants and product selection**

- Leaving the variant selector on for a product with no variants adds an unnecessary step for the customer. Turn it off in the settings when the product has no variants. [L0862.009]
- Variant options in Kaching Bundles: "Show variant selection for single quantity deal bar", "Hide theme variant picker", "Hide unavailable variant options", "Don't update other products when a variant is selected", "Set default variants" and "Add swatches". For a product with no variants, turn off "Let customers choose different variants for each item" so the selector does not appear. [L0862.034, L0861.003, L0862.001]
- Each bar selects its products via "All products", "Selected products" or "Selected collections", with a search filter. A counter shows how many products are selected out of the allowed maximum (200 in the example). [L0862.035]

**Visibility, schedule and states**

- Visibility and activity settings: Markets set to All, Exclude B2B customers, and the checkbox "Apply discount only via bundle widget", kept as shown in the default setup. Set Active dates (Start date, optional End date) only if the bundle has a limited run. [L0861.008, L0862.006]
- Active dates: the Other settings > Active dates section has Start date, Set end date and Start time fields. The start time is shown with the store's time zone (the example store showed GMT+9:30). Set both a start and an end date for limited-time bundles, and check the time zone before relying on the schedule. [L0861.038]
- Bundle deal states in the Kaching Bundles list: Active, Inactive and A/B test archive. Use Save as draft to keep changes unpublished and Publish to make the bundle live. [L0861.036]
- Mistake to avoid: leaving the variant selector on for a no-variant product, as above. [L0862.009]

### Bundle widgets on the product page (Rebuy and general)

**Principles**

- A dynamic bundle lets customers add several related products to the cart with one click. This shortens the path to a larger basket on the product page. [L0557.020]
- A dynamic bundle widget lets shoppers add several products to the cart with one click, and change the set by selecting or removing items first. Suggesting frequently bought together items, complete-the-look sets or curated product bundles reduces shopper decision fatigue and raises average order value. [L0559.001]
- Bundle offers on the product page can be built with the Kaching Bundles app. Use quantity tiers (for example buy 2 or buy 3 with a percentage off) and gift-style tiers (for example buy 3 and give 2 to someone). Place the bundle widget above the Add to Cart button so the offer is seen before purchase. [L1188.017]
- Use the same offer set across product pages and the cart, so the customer sees a consistent deal. Bundle apps can build buy-one-get-one-free, buy-X-get-Y-free and volume-discount offers. [L1411.014]

**Example data source (demo)**

- In the demo bundle, the Rebuy Mug ($10.00, White), T-shirt ($25.00, White / XS) and Bold Brew ($10.00, 1lb) add up to a total price of $45.00. The data source preview lists recommended items under the label "Frequently Bought Together" with their individual prices and a combined total, and the total is shown above the Add to Cart button before any discount is applied. [L0557.019, L0557.024]
- The bundle's total price is calculated from the included products and shown above the Add to Cart button before any discount. Discount settings sit in a separate Discount tab in the widget editor, so they can be adjusted without changing the product list. [L0557.025]

**Discount values in the widget**

- A Widget Discount only shows the saving visually on the bundle. It does not reduce the price in the cart. Real discounting requires either a Shopify Scripts discount (Shopify Plus only) or a Shopify automatic discount. [L0559.012]
- To set a discount per rule: open the rule, choose Widget Discount, select Percentage and enter the value. Each rule can have its own percentage. Rule titles should state the saving, so the shopper sees the discount in the bundle heading. [L0559.013]
- Setting one discount for a whole widget: if all bundles of one widget share the same discount, set it once in Widget Settings under the Discount field (for example 10% or 15%). Use rule-level Widget Discount percentages on the data source only when different bundles need different savings, and keep each percentage identical to the matching Shopify automatic discount. [L0559.014, L0559.027]
- Each bundle must be its own Shopify product, so bundle counts grow quickly. Five accessories with one bundle per accessory means at least five bundles, and every added combination multiplies that number. Keep the set small and limited to the best-selling accessories so shoppers are not overwhelmed. [L1357.049]

**Rebuy merchandising tools**

- ReBuy merchandising widgets include personalized product carousels, product add-ons and dynamic bundles. Add-ons and bundles are the offer types to use when raising order value on product pages and in the cart. [L0593.005]

### Page-builder embeds (PageFly and others)

- Integrate a bundle app such as White Bundle or BundleBear into PageFly pages:
  1. Find the HTML/Liquid section in PageFly.
  2. Copy the embed code from the bundle app.
  3. Place the code in the HTML section, positioned right after the "Add to Cart" button.
  4. The app widget will display on the product page.
  
  Different apps provide different code formats. Consult the app's documentation. [L0321.027]

### Rebuy Smart Cart: tiered progress bar and discount tiers

**Bar options and types**

- Smart Cart goal box > Tiered Progress Bar > Configure Tiers: each tier has a Choose Tier Type field (Free Shipping, free products, order discount, or decorative only), an Unlock Price field in dollars, and an Add New Tier button, with a maximum of four tiers. Advanced Bar Settings > Use Shopify Functions (Recommended) is on by default. The Free Gifts section has a Gift Area Title field. Separate Configure Icon and Configure Labels & Text sections change the bar's icon and wording. [L1230.026]
- Type list differs between sources. L1230.026 names "decorative only" as a type. L1231.025 and L1231.026 name Fixed Price. Check the tier-type list in your admin before you build. [L1230.026, L1231.025, L1231.026]
- A tiered progress bar supports up to 4 tiers. The tier types are Free Shipping, Free Product, Order Discount and Fixed Price. [L1231.026]
- Tiered Progress Bar with Shopify Functions can turn full-priced products into free gifts once a cart reaches a tier. Each tier can be set to a free gift, free shipping, an order discount, or a decorative stage. [L1230.012]
- Only one tiered progress bar is shown to a visitor at a time. Multiple bars can be built with different country limits. Example: one bar offers free shipping and a free gift in the US, while another offers only the free gift in Canada. [L1231.027]
- Each progress bar must have its countries specified, or it will show for all visitors. Currency settings follow the country codes assigned to each bar. [L1231.028]

**Setup procedure**

- Set up a tiered progress bar:
  1. In the cart editor, click Add Component to Header and choose Tiered Progress Bar, placing it in the header or goal box area.
  2. Set the countries where the bar should appear, using the country field with autocomplete. This replaces the old two-letter ISO code entry.
  3. Add a tier and choose its type: Free Shipping, Free Product, Order Discount, or Fixed Price.
  4. Set the threshold amount for that tier.
  5. For Free Product, choose the product and the variant option, then set the unlock price.
  6. Turn on Use Shopify Functions if the tier should be applied as a discount automatically.
  7. Set a custom tier label and icon size if needed.
  8. Repeat for additional tiers.
  9. Save the cart. [L1231.025]

**Free gifts and line items**

- Line item mode is a toggle for free gifts. When it is on, the free gift is added to the cart as a normal line item instead of being shown in the free gift area. This saves space and avoids a complex workaround. [L1231.030]
- For a Free Product tier, you can select a single variant or all variants of a product. When several variants are selected, the customer chooses one from a dropdown. With line item mode on, the chosen variant appears as a line item in the cart. [L1231.031]
- Any product used in a free product tier must be active, in stock and available for sale on the online store. Otherwise the reward cannot be applied at checkout. [L1308.003]
- Free Product tier example: a free product is added to the cart automatically when the cart reaches a $100 unlock price. The product is a single-variant item in the demo. [L1231.035]
- Use a custom tier label to describe the reward generically, such as savings or money off, instead of naming the free product or the discount amount. Icon size can also be set for each tier. [L1231.036]
- Selectable gift with purchase, where the customer picks the free gift, is not a native option on non-Plus stores at the time of the webinar (dated statement, Source E 2025-26 source). It needs extensive workarounds, a third-party app that can run Shopify Functions discounts, or a cross-sell widget. [L1231.037]

**Discount tiers (Shopify Functions)**

- Discount tiers can only be powered by Shopify Functions. When you add a discount tier, the Use Shopify Functions toggle is hidden. If you switch that setting off, the discount tier disappears for the user. [L1308.013]
- The step 6 wording in the setup procedure presents Use Shopify Functions as a choice. For discount tiers, keep it on, because L1308.013 says discount tiers depend on it. [L1231.025, L1308.013]
- Path to add a discount tier: Tiered Progress Bar > Configure Tiers > Add New Tier. For a discount tier, choose Order Discount Type (Percentage or fixed amount), set Order Discount Amount from Total, and set the Unlock Price (the cart total that unlocks the tier). [L1308.014]
- Order Discount tier example: spend $500 and get 25% off the order. Fixed Price tier example: spend $500 and get $50 off the order. Both are applied through Shopify Functions. [L1231.034]
- Example pair: a fixed $50 off tier unlocked at a $500 cart total, plus a percentage tier of 20% off unlocked at a $1000 cart total. Set the Order Discount Text of each tier to match its discount, such as "20% OFF". [L1308.015]
- By default, Functions discounts are allowed to combine with product, shipping and order discount types. In the tested cart, only the largest qualifying order discount was applied. With several tiers reached, the biggest one wins. [L1308.016]
- With a $50 fixed tier at $500 and a 20% tier at $1000, a cart of about $1000 or more receives only the 20% discount, because it is greater than the $50 fixed amount. The checkout shows only that order discount. [L1308.017]
- Remaining-amount label: with a $500 unlock price, a cart subtotal of $499.99 shows the remaining-amount label as one cent away from the reward. The remaining-amount placeholder always displays the gap between the current cart total and the next unlock price, so customers see exactly how little they still need to add. [L1308.025]
- Two product tiers: Tier 1 at a $50 unlock price for one free product and Tier 2 at a $100 unlock price for a second free product. When both tiers are unlocked, checkout shows both products fully discounted. The product discount text comes only from Tier 1, so keep that Tier 1 text generic so it fits both rewards. [L1308.026]

**Subscriptions and Buy More Save More**

- Switch to Subscription (Cart Items > Switch to Subscription) lets customers upgrade one-time products to subscriptions directly in the cart. It is useful when a store sells both one-time and subscription versions of a product. [L1230.014]
- Buy More Save More (Cart Items > Buy More Save More) is a tiered discount that encourages customers to buy more units of a product to unlock a bigger discount. [L1230.015]
- Set up subscription upgrades in the cart:
  1. Integrate your selling plan provider, such as Recharge, in the Rebuy admin under the Integrations tab.
  2. Open the cart body and toggle subscriptions on for the eligible line items.
  3. Customers who add a one-time version will see the option to switch to a subscription.
  4. Optionally turn on Disable downgrade to stop customers switching back from subscription to one-time.
  5. Edit the switch-to-subscription labels to fit your brand. [L1231.039]
- The Disable downgrade toggle stops customers from switching a subscription back to a one-time purchase. Leave it off if you want to keep the option available, since a step back can keep the journey moving. [L1231.040]
- Switch to subscription works across multiple selling plans for the same product. If the delivery frequencies share a uniform option, such as switching a 6-week item to a 4-week option, both items can be delivered together. [L1231.041]
- Buy More Save More is set up in the cart with editable language. At the time of the webinar it had no Shopify Function equivalent, so it is set up with Shopify automatic discounts or a third-party app instead. It was largely a Shopify Plus feature (dated statement, Source E 2025-26 source). [L1231.042]
- At the time of the webinar, Buy More Save More in Rebuy Smart Cart had no Shopify Function equivalent and had to be built with Shopify automatic discounts or a third-party app. Rebuy planned a Shopify Functions integration for this offer as its next one, but did not expect it before the end of that year (dated statement, Source E 2025-26 source). [L1231.060, L1231.042]
- ReBuy's smart cart shows a tiered progress bar and can switch the buyer to subscription options. Both elements can be fully customized to the brand, so the tiers and subscription offer can be set per store. [L0593.006]

### Cart progress bars (Kaching Cart)

- Kaching Cart > Cart settings > Promotions progress bar: set Reward type to Order discount and Discount type to Percentage. Set a Min price in USD for each tier. Use "Add tier" for more tiers. Optionally exclude specific products from being counted, or celebrate unlocks with confetti. [L0560.018]
- Set the promotion threshold above the price a customer must reach for buying two products, so a 5 percent discount requires buying two items. The lesson's example threshold is 50 dollars. [L0560.019]
- The progress bar can hold several reward tiers, for example a 5 percent discount and a higher 15 percent tier. Each tier needs its own threshold above the previous one. [L0560.020]
- Promotion progress bar texts (app placeholders kept as shown):

```
Default text: Add ((progress_needed)) to unlock ((next_promo_label))
Unlocked text: ((unlocked_promo_title)) Unlocked!
All promotions unlocked text: all rewards are unlocked
Promo discount text: #1 - ((promo_discount_amount)) discount
Discount text: ((promo_discount_amount)) discount
```

[L0560.021]

### Bundles and promotions without an app

- Run a bundle promotion without an app (older, 2022 source):
  1. During the promotion window, edit the existing product page.
  2. Describe the bundle in the title or description, for example "standing desk plus chair".
  3. Restore the normal product page after the window ends. [L1281.038]
- Create a bundle as a separate product page, or temporarily change an existing page, then remove it when the promotion ends. Orders from several suppliers are not automated, so each order has to be sent out as it arrives. [L1204.036]
- A bundle can be added as a selectable option on the existing product page, with extra description explaining what the upgraded package includes. Ads then keep sending the same visitors to the same page (older, 2021-22 source). [L1456.042]
- Alternatively, the bundle can be its own product page and feed item, using the same product title plus a suffix so it still shows for the standard product's search terms (older, 2021-22 source). [L1456.042]
- Whichever option is chosen, the price changes with the selection near the add-to-cart button. [L1456.042]

### Other bundle apps and names

- Bundle-building apps mentioned in the course: Bundle Bear for volume and buy-to-save offers. A second app is named differently across the sources (Bundler Where, White Bundle, or White Bundles / Bundle Where). Check the Shopify App Store for current options. [L1259.031, L1260.023, L0321.027]
- Coaching Bundles app (Shopify App Store) sets up offers such as multi-pack bundles on the product page. Build the competitor's offer structure in this app instead of coding it manually. [L0600.017]
- The Sketching Bundle app is a cheaper alternative to Bundle Bear. [L0635.026]

## Upsells, cross-sells and add-ons on the product page and cart

### Definitions and core principles

- An upsell offers a comparable higher-end or premium version of the item the customer is already buying, or adds value to it. It is presented at checkout or after purchase, when intent is already established. [L0240.013, L0434.011, L1412.002]
- A cross-sell offers a different, related or complementary item that enhances the main purchase, such as a paddle sold with a board. Cross-sell is what happens when a customer is asked whether they also want a matching accessory, not a more expensive version. [L0240.008, L0434.011, L1076.026, L1412.002]
- Upselling means persuading a customer who already wants a product to buy a higher-priced or upgraded version. The goal is more revenue and margin from the same buyer through extras that improve the experience, not new customers. [L1411.001]
- Cross-selling means offering a related or complementary product next to the item the customer is already buying, so they add it to the cart or buy both together. Typical placements are a "frequently bought together" block or a "customers also bought" section. [L1411.002]
- Upsells and cross-sells both aim to raise average order value (AOV) and profit margin. An upsell adds more value from the same customer, usually the same or a higher-priced version of the item. A cross-sell adds a complementary or supplementary product from the same niche. [L1410.002]
- A cross-sell is an offer for a different item than the main product. An upsell is an offer that adds value or a higher version of the item in the order. [L0434.011]
- In e-commerce the classic upsell term is often replaced by "reselling to the same customer". Classic upselling means moving a buyer from a cheaper product to a larger or more expensive one, such as a $100 item to a $1,000 item. [L0448.003]
- Upselling in e-commerce means increasing the money earned from each existing customer, not squeezing them. It works because the customer came to solve a problem, and a second product can solve it better or solve a related problem they commonly have. [L0448.001]
- Upsells and cross-sells raise AOV and profit margin. They work for every store, even one that sells a single product, and should be added at some level. [L1410.001]
- Upselling is judged by margin as well as price. A higher-margin item that customers accept can outweigh a more expensive item with thin profit. The upsell should be a better or more suitable product for the customer, not a pressure tactic. [L1076.036]
- Products that feel like a natural add-on convert better than random extras. Upsell products should complement or supplement the main product so the buyer sees an obvious use for them. [L1411.009]
- Cross-selling is a different item offered in the cart at a reduced price against its regular price. Shoppers see a clear saving on the add-on, while the store earns extra margin on each order. [L0435.015]
- Cross-sell offers should imitate good in-store sales help: suggest only items that actually go with the purchase and fit the customer's need. Showing a large pile of extras at once overwhelms the buyer and reduces conversion. [L1076.027]
- Low-ticket products can be offered as add-ons on a main product page instead of creating separate product pages for them. [L0835.055]

### Value, purpose and the Value Equation

- To create compelling upsells, solve for Perceived Value = Dream Outcome / (Effort + Time + Sacrifice). Example: a supplement upsell of $500 offers the dream outcome (focus, entrepreneurial success, accountability), reduces effort and time (1-on-1 VA coaching, done-with-you approach), and removes doubt (money-back guarantee). Position upsells as faster paths to the desired outcome than the base product alone. [L0307.018]
- Offer complementary products at cart level or inside a bundle when they solve a related need for the same buyer. Shoppers who buy a main product for a specific purpose are more likely to add a closely related accessory than an unrelated item. [L0435.022]
- Bundle upsells and cross-sells of another product that solves a similar problem can raise AOV. This works well in markets where the product problem is common. [L1171.026]
- Accessories that complement the main product, such as a coaster or metal straw for a coffee product, can work well as add-on bundle items. [L1383.052]
- Customers already buying the core product are most likely to add items they need to use it. Proven pairs include snowboard with snowboard bag, trampoline with safety net, fireplace with fireplace screen, and camera with memory card. [L0923.042]
- In a beauty niche with mostly female buyers, a related beauty tool, such as a facial massage set, works well in the cart. The offer should come from the same niche and speak to the same audience as the main product. [L1412.061]
- Only add cross-sell items that customers buy together with the core product, such as ottomans with chairs. Do not add unrelated bulky items such as rugs to a sofa store unless the supplier sells them as a set with the main product. [L0665.124]
- Cross-selling and upselling are about relevance. A cross-sell that is not logically tied to the main product gets ignored. [L0973.009, L0973.010]
- A "frequently bought together" block under or beside the main product is a cross-sell format: a related item is offered in one tap next to what the shopper is already viewing. [L1410.039]
- Marketplace product pages often show a "frequently bought together" block listing complementary items for a combined purchase. A drop-shipping store can copy this as a cross-sell by showing one or two matching products next to the main item at checkout. [L1412.054]

### Choosing relevant add-ons

- Use the Product Upsell Planner spreadsheet to identify which of your best-selling products have complementary items available as upsells. [L0203.001]
- Identify which products have high quality and high margins. Determine which services competitors offer offline (installation, consultation, buyers clubs). Discover non-tangible offerings (warranties, memberships) that customers would genuinely value. [L0240.015]
- Look for products where a better-fitting comparable item exists, and focus there. Upsell options should be shown where they make sense, not on every product. [L1076.037]
- Ask suppliers which add-on or upsell opportunities they can offer. Suppliers often know which accessories and options sell well with their products. [L0920.020]
- For each top product, ask suppliers which accessories sell well with it, since some of the best-selling add-ons are ones you would not expect. [L1357.020]
- Accessories work as upsells and downsells at checkout or after a sale. They are one of the simplest ways to raise margins in an existing store. [L1152.007]
- Upselling and cross-selling are most useful when the store has a clear core product. Once the store focuses on one product type, add recommended products, add-ons and upsells to the product pages, since these make it easier to increase order value. [L1346.022]
- In a competitive niche, maximising AOV matters most: add-ons and bundles can turn unprofitable items into profitable ones. The extra profit from accessories can let you outbid competitors who only break even on the main product. [L1357.019]
- A "frequently bought with" section and bundle sets lift AOV. An $8 product can reach about $60 per order through a subscription plus add-ons, which is what makes paid traffic profitable. [L0524.027]
- Upsells and cross-sells can increase profit margins on a low-priced TikTok product. They raise AOV without raising the main product price too far. [L1382.015]
- Upsell and cross-sell offers can raise the profit margin per order when the core product is priced in the sweet spot. [L1436.023]
- Offer a second item in a different colour or variant, since buyers often purchase two at once. This raises the value of each order without raising ad cost. [L0661.026]
- Customers who buy one unit of a product that suits multiple units can be offered a second unit at a discount. [L0448.005]

### Types of upsell

- There are five types of e-commerce upsell: multiple purchases of the same product, add-on accessories, more expensive products, related products, and bundles of several products. [L0448.004]
- **Multiple-purchase upsell:** offer the customer who is already buying product X a second unit at a discount on the second item, e.g. "buy two, get a discount on the second one". It works for products that suit multiple units and does not fit every catalogue, so other types are needed for the rest. [L0448.005]
- **Add-on upsell:** offer an accessory that enhances the core product and makes it solve the customer's problem better. Example pattern: a phone plus headphones that allow private listening at a comfortable volume. [L0448.006]
- **More expensive product:** an upsell to a more expensive product is allowed and not limited to cheaper add-ons. A customer buying a $500 product can be offered a $1,000 complementary product as a second purchase, with or without a special deal. [L0448.007]
- **Related-product upsell:** when customers share a passion or project that needs several products, offer a product that is not a direct add-on but serves that project. Example pattern: a grill buyer building an outdoor kitchen is offered an outdoor fridge. [L0448.008]
- Related-product offers do not have to go to every customer. Only the proportion of buyers who want the second product matters; the others may ignore the offer without harm. [L0448.009]
- **Bundle upsell:** after a first purchase, offer a set of three or four products, often lower-priced add-ons possibly mixed with a more expensive item, presented as working well together and sold at a combined price lower than buying separately. [L0448.010]
- An upsell can be a bundle that combines a core product with related items at one combined price, or an add-on accessory sold alongside it. Both formats work in the same on-site positions, so choose the format for each core product rather than using one format across the whole store. [L1409.032]
- Replace-item upsells are for an upgrade of the same product line, such as a premium fabric with a longer warranty. State the price difference clearly. Do not offer a competitor brand, because that pushes the customer away from the purchase. [L1076.041]
- A comparison chart can act as a light buyer guide on a product page. It shows the standard version next to an upgraded version, for example with a longer warranty or stronger material, and pushes buyers toward the upgrade. [L0664.054]
- A comparison chart upsell works best when two versions of one product differ on one visible specification, such as water capacity or cup size. The chart should show only the differences, so the buyer who viewed the cheaper model can see why the larger one fits better, without criticising the cheaper option. [L1076.078]
- Non-tangible upsells, such as extended warranties, installation services, design consultations or a buyer's club, add value without adding physical stock. Look at what top offline competitors offer as upsells and bring similar services to the online store. [L1076.053]
- Intangible offers are add-on services that cost the store little, such as an extended warranty, same-day shipping or white glove delivery. [L0923.046]

### Rollout: the 80/20 rule and timing

- Apply the 80/20 rule to cross-selling: add cross-sell options first to your best-selling products, then expand to lower-volume items as you refine the approach. [L0240.010]
- Start with the top 20 percent of products by sales, get those working, then roll the setup across the rest of the store. [L1076.030]
- Upsell and cross-sell by focusing on the top 20 percent of products and suggesting one add-on at a time that fits that product and customer. Results vary from no gain to a large lift, such as a 30 percent rise in AOV for those products. [L1113.077]
- Offer add-on options for your top 20 to 30 products where possible. [L1357.020]
- Sources differ on how many products to start with:
  - Source B (2021-22, older advice) starts with the most-sold products from a report, entering up to 20 core products in the planner. [L0448.021]
  - Source A sets the first scope at the top 20 percent of products by sales. [L1076.030, L1113.077]
  - Source A sets the scope at the top 20 to 30 products. [L1357.020]
  - Source C says to test the single product first and add complementary products later. [L0708.009]
- Timing differs by store stage. Sources differ:
  - In a brand-new store, do not start cross-selling early. Focus first on getting authorised to sell with the best brands in your core niche, optimising your ads and building the store fully. Add cross-sells once the core store is in place. [L1083.004]
  - Cross-selling is a feature to add as the store grows, not something to set up from the first day. [L0973.010]
  - Cross-sell early when the supplier already offers items that complete the same collection as your main product. This applies because you are already authorised to sell those items, competitors are likely bundling them, and customers want the matching set. [L1083.005]
  - Upsells and cross-sells work for every store, even one that sells a single product, and should be added at some level. [L1410.001]
  - For a start, test the single product first and add complementary products later. Refill or subscription items, such as replacement brushes, are an example of add-ons to add afterwards. [L0708.009]
- After the core niche is established and the store is built out and optimised, expand the cross-sell options further. Adding more options earlier spreads focus before the basics are working. [L1083.008]
- Example: a main dining set priced at 2,500 USD could be paired with a matching rug at an extra 800 USD and a matching buffet at an extra 1,200 USD. Because these items are part of the same collection, they are strong early cross-sell candidates. [L1083.006]
- Upsell and cross-sell by focusing on the top 20 percent of products (see above). [L1113.077]
- Average order value of about 350 dollars can be raised by adding closely related higher-price products, not by unrelated categories. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0962.108]
- Use Shopify product upsell app rules to show offers that depend on the product, such as a snowboard bag for a snowboard buyer. [L0923.039]

### Upsell planner sheets

- Upsell planner sheet fields per core product: Core Product Title, Special Offer? (a Paused column is also present), Type Of Upsell, and Upsell Product 1, Upsell Product 2, Upsell Product 3. Upsell product names must match existing product titles in the Shopify store. (Older advice, Source B 2021-22.) [L0448.016]
- Plan at most three upsell products per core product. One or two is fine, and there is no single correct number. [L0448.017]
- For each upsell, record the type (multiple, add-on, more expensive product, related product or bundle) and whether a special offer applies. Upsell products are not paid for with ads, so a discount can be offered even on items normally not discounted. [L0448.018]
- Build one upsell planner list first, pairing each core product with its matching upsell products. The same list then drives the on-site app settings, a separate email flow for each core product, and the script for customer service agents, so all three channels stay consistent. [L1409.033]
- Upsell offers are delivered on site, by email or during customer service, not by paid ads. Because acquisition cost was already paid on the first order, the upsell carries no customer acquisition cost. [L0448.019]
- A special offer on an upsell adds urgency and feels like a deal made just for the customer. A bundle can carry its discount built in, since the overall cost falls when several products are sold together. [L0448.020]

**Upsell planning procedure (Source B, 2021-22, older advice)**

1. Log into Shopify and pull a most-sold-products report, or use the equivalent report in Google Analytics. [L0448.021]
2. Enter the exact titles of up to 20 core products in the planner. [L0448.021]
3. For each core product, enter up to three upsell products by exact Shopify title. [L0448.021]
4. Enter the upsell type. [L0448.021]
5. Record whether a special offer applies. [L0448.021]
6. Keep the sheet for the next step, planning how the upsell is presented. [L0448.021]

**Setting up the BSGR Product Upsell Planner Google Sheet (Source B, 2021-22, older advice)**

1. Open the sheet from the link under the video; do not edit the shared original. [L0448.022]
2. Go to File > Make a copy to store an editable version in Google Drive. [L0448.022]
3. Alternatively, go to File > Download > Microsoft Excel (.xlsx) to edit it offline. Duplicate the copy as often as needed. [L0448.022]

### Conditional logic and triggers

- Use conditional logic to show a related option only after the customer selects a specific item. For example, show a car rack after a paddle is chosen, and a bag after a car rack is chosen. This walks the customer through a process rather than showing every option at once. [L1076.035]
- For a paddleboard store, logical cross-sell steps are the paddle, then a car rack after the paddle is chosen, then a bag after the car rack, and a rash guard as a further option. Each step depends on the item the customer already selected, so the offers follow the buying process rather than appearing all at once. [L1076.077]
- Configure triggers based on the product the customer is viewing and what is in their cart, so each customer sees a targeted offer. [L0923.039]
- A single store can hold several conditional funnels triggered by different products, so each product gets its own flow. Free-shipping thresholds and add-on suggestions are configured as multiple conditions. [L1410.026]
- Low-ticket add-ons can be shown based on what is already in the cart. [L0835.055]
- Cross-sell suggestions can be tied to tags in the theme (see Tools and setup). [L0973.008, L0973.018]

### Hidden add-on products

- Add-on products are set up as product pages that are not placed in any collection, not linked in navigation, and not included in product feeds or ads. They appear only as a drop-down add-on on the main product page. (2023 advice.) [L1283.078]
- Low-priced accessories that should only be sold as add-ons can be set to hidden in Shopify, so they stay out of the catalogue and search. They can still be attached to a high-ticket product through an add-on setup. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1281.040]
- Cheap add-ons, such as a paddle for a paddleboard, should not be listed as separate products or advertised. Show them only as add-ons and upsells, so they are bought only together with the main item. (Older advice, 2020.) [L1277.084]
- Low-ticket accessories priced between 10 and 200 dollars are best offered as cross-sells on high-ticket product pages, not promoted separately or sold through a PDF catalogue. [L0857.027]
- Supplemental low-priced products: if offered with a high-ticket item, the ideal scenario is that both ship from the same supplier, with no extra shipping fee. If separate shipping is required, use the product only as an add-on or upsell on the high-ticket product page, not as a standalone product with paid traffic. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0268.024]
- Example: a high-ticket bow at $800 and a repeat-purchase consumable, arrows at $100 per 20-pack. List the arrows as an add-on upsell for bow buyers, and separately offer them with organic traffic only for remarketing. Do not pay for traffic to the arrow page. Exception: if the repeat-purchase rate is high, the arrows can be included as a bundled product page. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0268.025]

### Product page upsells and add-ons

- Show upsells as attachments or add-ons to the main product, framed as benefits within the product section, so they feel native and do not interrupt the main purchase path. [L1016.034]
- Place add-on offers on the product page near the add-to-cart button, with a prompt asking whether the customer wants to add the item to the order and showing its price. Example: a $149 accessory bag offered beside a $1,000 paddleboard. [L1147.016]
- Place bundles directly above the description, under the buy button, and add upsells at checkout. One example offered a second pair with 10 percent off, and another offered priority processing at a higher price. [L0352.028]
- Downsells and upsells placed high on the product page increase AOV more than those placed low on the page. Bundles work best when visible early. [L0352.030]
- The main product is sold with the option of adding cheaper related items, such as necklaces or jewellery, directly on the product page. The upsells are shown as add-ons next to the main item instead of on the homepage, so the store stays focused on its core category. Example 1: a bag product page offering a matching wallet and a keychain as add-ons at checkout. Example 2: a candle product page offering a matching wick trimmer and a gift box as add-ons on the same page. [L0838.012]
- Keep the base price in the listing and offer paid add-ons on the product page, so the total rises only when the customer chooses extras. The formula is: low base price in ads, optional add-ons at checkout, total increases at the point of decision. This works because customers click on the price they searched for and only then see the extras. (2018 source.) [L1109.033]
- Example: a $600 camping chair listed at $600 in Google Shopping, with a $40 cooler bag, a $25 cushion and a $15 carry bag offered as add-ons on the product page. The total can reach $680 only if the customer chooses them. (2018 source.) [L1109.034]
- When a key accessory is not included with the product, do not add it to the base price. Offer it as an optional add-on with a yes or no choice and its own price on the product page. [L1130.031]
- Keep the main product page free of unrelated extras, since they add friction. [L1345.101]
- Once the store focuses on one product type, add recommended products, add-ons and upsells to the product pages. [L1346.022]
- Upsell options that work in almost any niche are expedited shipping, extended warranties, and protective or accessory products. For a sofa, examples are a leather protectant, an anti-slip pad, or an upgrade to real leather. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0854.035]
- Add-on products such as extended warranties or maintenance plans can be sold through the Bold Product Options app. The add-on price appears directly on the product page and the customer adds it to the order there. Alternatives are separate add-on product pages or draft orders. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1281.039]
- Configuring one cross-sell product in Bold Product Options takes about 10 to 20 minutes. The interface is simple and needs no coding. [L1076.033]
- Example: a store selling pellet grills can offer these accessories alongside the main grill: utensils, pellets, cedar planks, thermometers, smoking racks, grill covers, brisket slicing knives, marinades and rubs, meat shredding claws and cast iron cookware. Pellets, planks and rubs are consumables that bring the customer back for repeat orders. (Source B 2021-22, older advice.) [L0511.023]

### Cart upsells and cart drawer

**Cart upsell setup in Visually (Source E 2025-26)**

1. Add a Products Upsells widget below the last product in the cart. [L0389.010]
2. Choose the Blocks List layout (vertical list). [L0389.010]
3. Show three products only, without a Show More button. [L0389.010]
4. Place Add to Cart on the right, move the zoom button to the bottom left, and show product reviews above the product name. [L0389.010]
5. Add a heading such as "Our Personal Favorites", set the block spacing to 10 on both sides, make the reviews black, set the Add to Cart border radius to 0, and make the zoom icon background transparent with no border or shadow. [L0389.010]
6. Choose the Personalized algorithm and the filters listed below. [L0389.010]
7. Set Audience to All Visitors, Pages to Cart page, Device to Mobile, name the test (for example "mobile cart sales"), add AOV as a goal, tick the box and publish. [L0389.010]

**Visually layouts and algorithms**

- Visually upsell layouts: Blocks List shows a vertical list of blocks; Columns splits the space evenly for up to 3 upsells; Boxes Slider is a scrollable list of upsells; Blocks Slider is a horizontal slider of blocks; Grid shows two or more columns of upsells. Choose the layout that fits the cart space and the number of products to show. [L0389.011]
- Visually upsell algorithms include Manually (choose the items), Personalized (based on the customer's behaviour), Advanced Rules (custom logic), New Arrivals, Viewed Together, Viewed With Recently Viewed, Purchased Together, Most Popular (sold most in the past 30 days), Recently Viewed, and Purchased With Recently Purchased. Personalized was chosen as the AI-powered default for a cart upsell. [L0389.012]

**In-stock and price filters**

- Filter cart upsells so they show only items that are in stock, not already in the cart, and priced above zero. Without the price filter, a free add-on such as a free loop can appear as an upsell. [L0389.013]
- A free product offered as a cart upsell can take the top spot and confuse the shopper, so upsell filters must exclude items with a price of zero. Check the upsell block in the cart preview before publishing to confirm no free item appears. [L0389.014]
- When running sitewide markdowns, review which products the cart recommendation block shows, because recommendations should be chosen deliberately for the promotion rather than left to default logic. [L1231.006]

**Cart drawer and post-add-to-cart upsells**

- Enable a slide-out cart that shows complementary products with the Monster Cart Upsell app, and add a free-shipping threshold, for example free shipping over $50, to encourage buyers to add a cheap item such as a $20 product. [L0871.045]
- A back-pain store's cart slide-out offers complementary items next to the main product, such as a back stretcher, a neck product, a second neck product and a pain relief cream. Every buyer sees a logical next add-on at checkout. [L0871.090]
- Set up a post-add-to-cart upsell sequence before checkout, with a visible skip button on each step. Example: deodorant offered at £12 and hair care at £24, both skippable. [L0524.026]
- A slider add-to-cart block or a pop-up upsell offering an upgraded bundle can be added after a visitor adds an item to the cart. This is useful for stores with many products because it presents more options. [L1345.101]
- Pre-checkout upsells have two standard placements: the product page, where buy-one-get-one and bundle offers are most common, and the cart page, where add-ons, free-shipping goals and extra items fit best. [L1410.038]
- Cart drawer threshold messages push customers to add more to reach a reward, such as adding 10 more items for free shipping or 13 more items for 20% off. Another variant is adding a $30 item to unlock free shipping. [L1411.016]
- Tiered cart offers from the lesson, using Monster Cart Upsell (values are examples; adjust to your prices): [L1412.017]

| Cart value | Offer shown |
|---|---|
| about 15 | free shipping |
| about 30 | free gift |
| about 60 to 70 | 20% off |

Other examples in the lesson: add 3.99 to get free shipping, and add 13.4 to unlock 20% off. [L1412.017]

- Sources differ on the free-shipping threshold, and the values depend on the store:
  - Monster Cart Upsell example: free shipping over $50. [L0871.045]
  - Tiered table: free shipping at a cart value of about 15. [L1412.017]
  - Lesson examples: add 3.99 for free shipping, or a $30 item to unlock it. [L1412.017, L1411.016]
  - All values are examples, so set the threshold to your own margin and shipping costs. [L1412.017]
- Pre-select low-priced optional extras in the cart, such as priority processing, shipping protection, extended warranty or gift wrapping. Keep each extra under 5 USD, for example 3.99 to 5.99 USD, because most buyers accept it without much thought. [L1412.018]
- Example: a 6-month extended warranty priced around 3.90 to 5.90 USD can be pre-selected in the cart. Pre-selecting it keeps the add-on small enough that most buyers leave it checked. [L1412.059]

### Pricing, margin and price limits

- Upsell products should be priced at around 20 to 30 percent of the core product price, and never above the core price. Keep upsells above roughly 50 dollars, because very low-priced add-ons are not worth the effort. [L0923.040]
- Sources differ on the upsell price ceiling:
  - Source A: upsell products are 20 to 30 percent of the core price and never above it. This applies to add-on upsells on the product page. [L0923.040]
  - Source B (2021-22, older advice): an upsell to a more expensive complementary product is allowed. A customer buying a $500 product can be offered a $1,000 complementary product as a second purchase, with or without a special deal. This applies to a second purchase of a related higher-priced product. [L0448.007]
- Sources differ on minimum prices for add-ons:
  - Source A: keep upsells above roughly $50. [L0923.040]
  - Source A: low-ticket accessories priced between 10 and 200 dollars are best offered as cross-sells on high-ticket product pages. [L0857.027]
  - Source A: cross-sell products under $200 as add-ons to orders of high-ticket items. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0469.072]
  - Source C: pre-selected cart extras stay under 5 USD (3.99 to 5.99 USD). [L1412.018]
  - Source C: a small ebook for about 5 to 10 dollars can be sold as a cross-sell alongside the main product. [L1381.043]
- Accessories must be profitable when offered in-store. For example, a $30 accessory at 30 percent margin yields $9, which is not enough if shipping costs $9. Either price the add-on for profit or make it yourself. [L0511.012]
- If the supplier's accessory is expensive, for example an $80 wholesale cost, do not give it away for free. Offer it as an add-on option priced close to wholesale cost, which still beats competitors that do not offer it. [L0894.035]
- When an add-on comes from a different supplier, check that the margin still works before offering it. [L1092.051]
- Offer upsell add-ons sourced from the same supplier as the main product, so shipping is bundled and simpler. If the same supplier has no suitable add-on, use an add-on from a different supplier. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1092.050]
- Lead value optimisation (LVO) raises AOV through product options, add-ons and upgraded shipping options. These are the tools to reach a 30% net margin when the base margin is thin. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0664.053]
- Lead value optimisation (LVO) adds margin on top of the MAP through cross-sells and upgrades, such as expedited shipping for a fee, extended warranties, and product add-ons. Revenue per order then becomes MAP plus cross-sells and upgrades, which widens the spread when prices rise. [L0942.019]
- Upsells, downsells and complementary accessories raise AOV and customer lifetime value (CLV). For a main product, the buyer typically also needs accessories and consumables, so these add-ons should be offered in the store. [L0511.011]
- Price tiers or upsells add value for low-frequency purchases, but the store should still be built around first-time buyers and their trust. (2023 advice.) [L1283.079]
- Example: a store adds a Gua Sha Facial Set to the cart as a cross-sell. The regular price was 69 USD and the cross-sell price 39 USD. The cart value for the same customer rose from 49 USD to 88 USD, with the same ad cost per customer. Because the store increased AOV and profit without extra acquisition cost, the offer worked. [L0434.016, L0435.016]
- Cross-sell by offering a product only available with a specific main purchase, at a discounted price. For example, a store selling necklaces can offer a matching ring at a discount, and the exclusivity makes the offer feel scarce. [L1399.009]
- Complementary cross-sell: offer a related product to customers who are already ready to buy, at a discounted price, so the order value rises. Making the add-on exclusive to a specific purchase adds scarcity and fear of missing out. Example: a customer buying a necklace is offered a matching ring at a reduced price, available only with the necklace. [L1400.006]
- Increase AOV with add-ons and upsells, and ask your suppliers which add-on or upsell opportunities they can offer. [L0920.020]
- The average order value of about 350 dollars can be raised by adding closely related higher-price products. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0962.108]
- Sources differ on what counts as a "good" conversion rate: well-chosen upsells can reach conversion rates of 20 percent or more. Test different offers, because one upsell can convert at about 5 percent while another, such as a snowboard kit with leash, stomp pad, wax and an iron for about 100 dollars, can reach around 30 percent. [L0923.041]
- A special offer on a bundle carries its discount built in, since the overall cost falls when several products are sold together. [L0448.020]

### Warranties and protection

- Adding extended warranties as a Shopify offer had a sales target of 5 extended warranties per month. [L1509.012]
- Warranty offers for electronics commonly run 12 months, one year or three years. Warranty exclusions such as theft and customer-inflicted damage should be stated in small print, so the terms are visible but easy to miss. [L1381.042]
- For an electronics product, a warranty can be added at checkout as a cross-sell rather than a separate upsell. Common terms are coverage for 12 months, one year or three years, with exclusions such as theft and customer-inflicted damage listed in small print. [L1381.061]
- A supplier that already offers a one-year warranty may agree to extend it to two or three years for an agreed price, while the store sets its own selling price. [L0923.046]
- Non-tangible offers such as extended warranties add value without physical stock. Expedited shipping, extended warranties and protective or accessory products work in almost any niche. [L1076.053, L0854.035]
- Sources differ on how to present a warranty:
  - Electronics: a warranty is offered at checkout as a cross-sell with 12-month, one-year or three-year terms. [L1381.061, L1381.042]
  - Low-priced optional extra: a 6-month extended warranty around 3.90 to 5.90 USD is pre-selected in the cart. [L1412.059]

### Gift options

- Offer optional gift wrapping at checkout for roughly $5 to $10, depending on product size. It adds perceived value and an unboxing moment at very low cost, but the 3PL (third-party logistics provider) must support it. [L1225.018]
- Add a gifting option to the product page with gift wrapping, a personal message at checkout and a gift flow, sold as an upsell, because a giftable product can benefit from a bespoke gifting experience. [L1016.051]
- Gift wrapping can be pre-selected in the cart as a low-priced optional extra, kept under 5 USD. [L1412.018]

### Finding complementary products

**Finding a complementary add-on product (Source C)**

1. Find your product on Amazon. [L1107.034]
2. Check the "Frequently bought together" section for product ideas. [L1107.034]
3. Research your customers to see what problems they have and what would help them. [L1107.034]
4. Offer the best-fitting product as an add-on. [L1107.034]

- Example: for a cleaning product, a useful add-on is an e-book with tips. You can buy a PLR (private label rights) e-book or make one yourself in Canva, but a bought e-book still needs a cover and pictures to look professional. [L1107.035]
